- Open issues: (1) the avatar slot is empty until Task 11 (superseded in Task 11). (2) The row height is fixed, so long previews are cut at the row edge (`Wrapping::None` plus `clip`), not with an ellipsis, beyond the 120-character cap. (3) The windowed range comes from the last reported scroll position; before the first scroll event it assumes an 800 px viewport (`DEFAULT_VIEWPORT_HEIGHT`). (4) A search is sent per keystroke (one command each, no debounce); the core sets the filter with `reset_to_one_page`.
- New human items (rule 9, not ticked by the agent): in the Phase 2 human checkpoint below.
- Open issue fixed (flaky `last_message_preview_shows_own_message`, failed in the Task 9 and Task 10 gate runs). Root cause: not a core bug and not a race with `listen_to_room`. The shared test accounts piled up rooms across runs (alice had 411 joined rooms; every run adds about 25). A core logging in as alice processes every joined room of the first sync, and matrix-sdk's event cache does that one room at a time at about 90 ms each in a debug build (visible in the `event_cache::states` lock timers: a steady ~10 room updates per second for ~40 s). The marker message's sync response is queued behind that work, so its latest-event `Set` arrived about 41 s after the room was listed, past the 30 s wait. Evidence: the test failed 4 of 6 runs of the file and 3 of 3 alone on the polluted account; with the wait raised to 150 s it passed with the preview arriving ~41 s after the send; the core's own `map_batch` cost was microseconds per batch (timed, not kept); no `Set` carried a preview before the marker because no latest event was computed until the event cache finished; read in matrix-sdk 0.19.1 `latest_events/mod.rs` (`listen_to_room`, `compute_latest_events_task`) and matrix-sdk-ui `room_list.rs` (`merge_stream_and_receiver` emits a `Set` per `RoomInfoNotableUpdate`): updates are delivered correctly, just late. No previews are lost in real use, only delayed on a first sync of a very large account in a debug build.
- Fix: new `scripts/prune-test-rooms.sh` (curl + jq; every test user leaves and forgets all joined rooms and declines all invites; prints counts only, never tokens or room names), called at the end of `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, which the gate runs before the ignored tests. It must run between test runs, not during one. No timeout, sleep or assertion was changed, so there is no Rust change and no unit test applies. After the fix: the file alone passed 5 of 5 (8/8 each, 10-18 s, with no pruning between the runs, so ~100 rooms accumulated); shellcheck (docker) clean on both scripts; full gate passes; the ignored run (`docker compose up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh && cargo test -p twicecat-core -p twicecat-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`) passed 3 times in a row, the first after `docker compose down -v`; then `docker compose stop`. Note for a human: a release build or a smaller account is far quicker; if previews on a first sync of a 2,000+ room account feel slow, that is the SDK's event cache, not the core.

## Phase 2 / Task 11 notes
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked`, `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check`, and the ignored run (`docker compose up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh && cargo test -p twicecat-core -p twicecat-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`, then `docker compose stop`): all pass (app: 166 lib tests, 5 bin tests; the ignored run: all suites ok, 0 failed).
- TDD: the `avatars` tests were written first and run against stubbed `decode_avatar` / `initials` (9 failed); the `update` tests were written before `App::avatars` existed (compile error), then with the field but no behaviour (7 failed), then the code. Mutation check: with the decoder limits removed, the limit-edge test fails.
- New `crates/app/src/avatars.rs`:
  - `decode_avatar(&[u8]) -> Option<Handle>`: avatar bytes come from other users, so they are decoded here, never passed raw to iced. Only PNG, JPEG and WebP are accepted (format guessed from the content); `Limits` are `max_image_width`/`max_image_height` = `MAX_AVATAR_SIDE` (512) and `max_alloc` = `MAX_DECODE_ALLOC` (8 MiB); the result is RGBA pixels in `Handle::from_rgba`, so iced never decodes untrusted bytes. Failure, a wrong format, a corrupt or truncated body, or a header over the limits gives `None`, which shows the initials placeholder. Tests: tiny valid PNG, 96 px, the exact limit edge (512 ok, 513 refused), JPEG, WebP, empty, garbage, truncated, damaged, GIF and SVG refused, and IHDR headers claiming 60000x60000, u32::MAX x 1 and 1 x 513 (CRC fixed up, tiny body).
  - `Avatars` (state in `App::avatars`): `requested: HashSet<AvatarId>` and `states: HashMap<AvatarId, AvatarState>` (`Ready(Handle)` / `Unavailable`). `take_unrequested` returns each not-yet-requested ID once (also within one batch) and marks it; at most `MAX_REQUEST_BATCH` (128) per command, the rest stays unrequested for a later change. `answer` ignores IDs never requested, stores `Ready` for a valid image and `Unavailable` for `None` or an image that fails validation. **Retry policy: none this session.** Every ID is requested once per session whatever the answer (in flight, answered, or answered `None`), so scrolling cannot cause a retry storm against a failing homeserver. The sets are cleared on `LoggedOut` and `SessionExpired` (`back_to_login`). Its `Debug` shows counts only (no MXC URIs).
  - `initials(name)`: takes the already-sanitised name (`room_list::row_name`), skips leading `#`, `@`, `!`, splits on whitespace and on the sanitiser's line-break mark, drops leading U+FFFD marks, and takes the first character of the first two words only if it is alphanumeric (so emoji, ZWJ sequences and flag halves never give a half glyph), uppercased with the first char of the uppercase mapping (`ß` gives `S`). Empty, blank, symbol-only or marker-only names give `"?"`, drawn on a neutral grey disc. Unit tests: plain, single word, `#room:server`, `@user:server`, `!abc:server`, accented, Cyrillic, CJK, emoji, hostile sanitised name, and a 200,000-character name.
  - `placeholder_color(room_id)`: FNV-1a of the ID into the 8-colour `PLACEHOLDER_COLORS` list (stable across Rust versions; unit-tested for stability, membership and spread).
- `App::request_avatars` runs after every `RoomListChanged`, `RoomListLoaded` and scroll (`after_list_change`, together with paging): it collects the `avatar` IDs of the built rows (`visible_range` with overscan) that were never requested and sends one `FetchAvatars`, or nothing if the set is empty. A query change comes back from the core as a list change, so it is covered. Tests: a `Reset` of 100 rooms with a 20-row viewport requests exactly rows 0..30 in one command; a repeat, a re-`Reset` and `RoomListLoaded` request nothing; scrolling requests only the new rows; no avatar sends nothing; a shared avatar is requested once; a changed avatar of a visible room is requested; scrolling back and forth five times over failed (`None`) avatars sends each ID exactly once; a `Clear` + `Reset` after `None` answers asks again for nothing; logout and expiry clear the state and a new session asks again.
- Q17 timing: `room_list_batches_under_16ms` still runs all four 2,000-room shapes through `update`, which now includes the avatar bookkeeping; each run also asserts that exactly one `FetchAvatars` was sent and that the bookkeeping is non-empty. Medians (debug, this machine, 15 runs after a warm-up): reset 2000 1.46 ms; 4 x append 500 1.39 ms; mixed on 2000 0.48 ms; clear then reset 2000 0.60 ms (budget 16 ms).
- View (`room_list_pane::avatar_view`): a 40 px round slot: the decoded image when `Ready`, otherwise the initials (white on the room's palette colour, or on the theme's muted grey for `"?"`). The placeholder stays when unavailable. A smoke test builds the element in every state.
- APIs used, confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/`:
  - image-0.25.10: `ImageReader::new` src/io/image_reader_type.rs:89, `format` :110, `limits` :137, `with_guessed_format` :254, `decode` :311 (applies the dimension limits and `limits.reserve(decoder.total_bytes())` before allocating); `Limits` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, fields `max_image_width`/`max_image_height`/`max_alloc`, so built from `Limits::default()` and assigned) src/io/limits.rs:30-43; `DynamicImage::into_rgba8` src/images/dynimage.rs:418; `ImageBuffer::dimensions` src/images/buffer.rs:705, `into_raw` :695; `ImageFormat::{Png, Jpeg, WebP}` src/io/format.rs:22.
  - iced_core-0.14.0: `image::Handle::from_rgba(width, height, pixels)` src/image.rs:147; `Handle`'s `Debug` omits the pixels (:185); `Text::color` src/widget/text.rs:169; `border::rounded` src/border.rs:24; `Color::from_rgb` (const) src/color.rs:74.
  - iced_widget-0.14.2: `image()` helper src/helpers.rs:1812 (behind the `image` feature, via `image-without-codecs`), `Image::new` src/image.rs:74, `.width` :91, `.height` :97, `.border_radius` :175; `Container::{width :121, height :127, center_x :145, center_y :150}` src/container.rs.
- Open issues (Phase 2 wrap-up list):
  1. iced text-input buffers are not zeroized (Q19): the password and composer text live in iced's own buffers while typed.
  2. Decoding untrusted thumbnails relies on the `image` crate (png, zune-jpeg, image-webp). It decodes through `image::ImageReader` with `Limits` (512 px, 8 MiB allocation) from at most 1 MiB of input (the core's cap), and runs inside `update` (a 512 px image decodes in a few milliseconds; the usual 96 px thumbnail much less). A decoder bug would be reachable by any user who sets an avatar in a room you see.
  3. The CLI and the app share one data dir and keychain entry (Q20); running both at once is unsupported (SQLite store lock).
  4. A failed avatar fetch is not retried this session (no backoff, by design); it shows initials until the next login or restart. A transient homeserver outage therefore leaves initials for those rooms.
  5. Avatars of rooms are only fetched for the built rows (visible plus 10 rows of overscan); a fast scrollbar drag can show initials for a moment before the thumbnails arrive.
  6. Initials ignore emoji-led words by design (no half glyphs); a name made only of emoji shows `?`.
- Phase 2 agent work is done. The human checkpoint below stays open. New human items (rule 9, not ticked by the agent): in the Phase 2 human checkpoint below.

## Phase 2 / Task 11 wrap-up review fixes
- Local-homeserver guard (rule 6): new `scripts/require-local-homeserver.sh` (sourced by `prune-test-rooms.sh` and `create-test-users.sh` after `.env.test`, before any request) exits 1 unless `HOMESERVER_URL` is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, `http://localhost:<port>` or `http://[::1]:<port>` (one optional trailing `/`). Demonstration, each run from a scratch copy of `scripts/` with a `.env.test` whose `HOMESERVER_URL` was replaced; every run printed `refusing to use HOMESERVER_URL: it must be http://127.0.0.1:<port>, ...` on stderr and made no request:
  - `HOMESERVER_URL=https://matrix.org`: `prune-test-rooms.sh` exit 1, `create-test-users.sh` exit 1.
  - `http://example.com:8008`, `http://127.0.0.1.evil.com:8008`, `http://localhost:8008@evil.com`, `http://127.0.0.1` (no port), `http://localhost:8008/x`: both scripts exit 1 each.
  - Control: `http://127.0.0.1:9` (loopback, nothing listening) passes the guard and then fails on the connection (exit 7 from curl for `prune-test-rooms.sh`).
- `prune-test-rooms.sh`: the password reaches jq through its environment (`env.PRUNE_PASSWORD`), not argv; a failed leave or forget is counted, no longer skips `/logout`, is reported on stderr, and makes the script exit non-zero after all three users were handled (`prune` is deliberately not called with `||`, which would disable `set -e` inside it; found while testing the first version). `create-test-users.sh`: the script directory is captured before `cd`, so the call to `prune-test-rooms.sh` no longer depends on a relative `$0`. `shellcheck -x scripts/*.sh` (docker `koalaman/shellcheck`) reports nothing.
- Rust tests: `common::is_local_homeserver` / `assert_local_homeserver` in `crates/core/tests/common/mod.rs` (called by `TestEnv::load`, so before any credentials are read or sent) and the same pair in `crates/app/tests/bridge.rs` (`TestEnv::load` and `http_json`); a non-ignored test per crate checks accepted and refused URLs (userinfo trick, suffix host, https, missing port, path).
- `text_safety`: `is_combining_mark` now covers U+0483-0489, U+0591-05BD, U+05BF, U+05C1-05C2, U+05C4-05C5, U+05C7, U+0610-061A, U+064B-065F, U+0670, U+06D6-06DC, U+06DF-06E4, U+06E7-06E8, U+06EA-06ED, U+0E31, U+0E34-0E3A, U+0E47-0E4E and the Vedic marks. Each range was checked against Unicode 15.0 general categories (Python `unicodedata`; no `unicode-*` crate in the lock file exposes the category): all are Mn/Me. Deviation: U+1CD0-1CFF is not covered whole, because that block also holds letters (Lo), punctuation and unassigned code points; only its Mn code points are counted (1CD0-1CD2, 1CD4-1CE0, 1CE2-1CE8, 1CED, 1CF4, 1CF8-1CF9). Newly dropped as invisible: U+17B4, U+17B5, U+180B-180F, U+1D173-1D17A, U+FFFC. New `name_or_placeholder` (`(unnamed)`) is used for room names, preview sender names and timeline sender names that are empty or only space, U+00A0, U+2000-200A or U+3000 after sanitising. Tests include `"a" + ("\u{0301}"*4 + "\u{0483}").repeat(50)` keeping 4 marks.
- Emoji and joiners: U+FE0E/FE0F and U+200C/200D are kept, one selector and one joiner per base character (a selector and a joiner do not use each other's allowance, so 👁️‍🗨️ survives); runs collapse. U+FE00-FE0D and U+E0100-E01EF are still dropped. Tag characters U+E0020-E007F are kept only right after U+1F3F4 and at most 8 of them; any other character, even a dropped one, ends the sequence. Tests: ❤️, 1️⃣, 👨‍👩‍👧, a Persian ZWNJ word and a Scotland-style flag are unchanged; 500 FE0F collapse to one; tags after a letter are dropped.
- Room selection: `RoomListChanged` deselects (and drops the draft and timeline) only while the query is the default; with a search or a non-default tab the open room and its draft stay even if the filtered list lacks the room (including a `Clear`). Logout, expiry and the default query still deselect. Tests cover search, tab, default query, return to the default query, logout and expiry.
- `pending_cancel` is cleared on `NoSession`, `LoggedOut` and `SessionExpired` (`back_to_login`). Test: `a_pending_cancel_is_forgotten_when_the_session_state_changes`.
- Checks: fmt, clippy (`--all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked`, `check-app-deps.sh`, `check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check`, shellcheck, and the ignored run against the local Synapse (`create-test-users.sh` with the new guard and prune, then `-p twicecat-core -p twicecat-app -- --ignored --skip keyring`; all suites ok, no flaky run) all pass; the keyring test was not run; `docker compose stop` afterwards.
- Open issues left by this step:
  1. `is_combining_mark` is still a hand-written list, not the full Unicode Mn/Me table: marks of scripts not listed (Devanagari, Bengali, Tamil, Thai tone marks outside the range and so on) still count as base characters and reset the counter, so a run alternating a listed and an unlisted mark is not capped. Closing this needs the full table (a generated range table or a crate such as `unicode-normalization`/`unicode-segmentation`, which needs approval as an app dependency). Output length is still bounded by the 256/4096 character caps.
  2. Selection keeps a hidden room only while a non-default query is active; a stale list update for the old query arriving just after the user returned to the default query can still drop the open room (the following `Reset` shows it again, but the draft is gone).
  3. The subdivision-flag rule keeps up to 8 invisible tag characters after any U+1F3F4, a small amount of smuggling room for a hostile sender, not checked against the valid flag tag list.
  4. A selector and a joiner each survive once per base, so a hostile string can carry two invisible characters per visible character within the length caps.

## Phase 2 open issues (summary)
- A DM seen before its `m.direct` account data stays non-DM and shows under the wrong tab (Task 4 notes).
- An OAuth flow can be left pending in the core in narrow races, refusing logins for up to 10 minutes (Task 9 notes).
- A delayed `CancelOAuth` may cancel a newer attempt (Task 9 notes).
- `text_safety` still uses hand-written ranges (Task 10 and Task 11 wrap-up notes): unlisted combining marks (Indic, Syriac, Tibetan, supplementary planes) bypass the cap; joiners and selectors after any base allow look-alike names; up to 8 tag characters survive after U+1F3F4; invisible-only names escape "(unnamed)".
- Avatar decoding runs on the UI thread without `catch_unwind` or cache eviction (Task 11 notes).
- Avatar fetches are thumbnail-only (Q11 deviation; Task 5 notes).
- The first sync of an account with hundreds of rooms takes about 40 s in debug because of SDK per-room work (Task 10 notes).
- No search debounce (Task 10 notes).
- `scripts/create-test-users.sh` puts the password in curl argv (local test homeserver only).
- The three copies of the loopback check (two Rust test helpers and `scripts/require-local-homeserver.sh`) must be kept in sync (Task 11 wrap-up notes).

## Phase 4 / Task 9 notes (log capture and data at rest, core side)
- Step done: `crates/core/tests/log_hygiene.rs` (own test binary, one ignored test, verbatim ignore reason), Q16 (c) unit tests in `paths.rs` and `session.rs`. `EncryptedPair` in `tests/common/mod.rs` now exposes the two cores' secret stores (`secrets`).
- What it checks: a global `tracing_subscriber` registry with two layers on one flow, `info` and `DEFAULT_FILTER`, each into its own in-memory buffer. The flow: alice and carol reset to B0, encrypted room, each core looks the peer up, sentinel bodies both ways, identity reset on alice's core 1, recovery enabled (key captured), alice's second core logs in, SAS between the two alice cores, `Recover` with the key on core 2, device rename, then the scans, then logout of all three cores and the log scans. 12 sentinel values: access token and store passphrase of the 3 stored records (no refresh token is present with password login), 2 passwords, the recovery key (with and without spaces), 2 bodies. UTF-8 and JSON-escaped forms are searched. Files under the 3 data roots (39 files, about 8 MB, -wal/-shm included) are scanned while the sessions are live; the logs after logout.
- Result: no sentinel in the logs (both filters) and none on disk. The test prints only counts, kinds and file names.
- Self-check: first, a sentinel logged with `tracing::info!` into a separate discarded buffer and one written to a scratch file (`nested/file.db-wal`) must be found, an unrelated sentinel must not. Seen red: with the `info!` line removed the self-check failed ("the capture or the scanner missed the self-check sentinel").
- Order differs slightly from Q16 (a): SAS first, then `Recover` on core 2 (a recovered device is already verified, so a self-verification request after it would test a different path). Bodies are sent before the identity reset.
- Flake found and fixed in the test (not the core): sending right after the reset Security event sometimes left alice's own message unsent for 5 to 60 s (3 of 14 runs; the first run after `create-test-users.sh` failed twice at the 30 s pump timeout), and 8/8 runs passed with a 5 s pause there. The cause is the restore window of `SyncPause` (sync restarting, send queue off) right after the event; the test now sends the bodies before the reset and retries `EnableRecovery` on `Busy`. Not fixed in the core: a message sent in the seconds after `ResetIdentity` completes can be delayed (Task 10/12 should not assume it goes out at once). After the change 10/10 runs passed, plus the two official runs below.
- Ignored runs right after `scripts/create-test-users.sh`: first 19.33 s, repeat 18.86 s, both green (earlier runs 16 to 22 s).
- Unit tests (Q16 c): `paths.rs` lists the data root after `create_session_store_dir` + `write_last_account` (only `last_account` and `sessions`, and `sessions/` holds only the store dir); `session.rs` checks that oversize/error strings carry no sentinel and that `Serialize` and `serde_json::to_*` appear in no other source file. Both were written after the code and passed at once (they pin existing behaviour).
- Gate: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` all pass.
- Residual (for the Task 14 review): SDK `debug`/`trace` are not asserted; table layout and sizes of the SQLite store stay visible; the scan covers values the test knows, not derived forms (base64, URL-encoded).
- Nothing new for the human checkpoint.

### Phase 4 / Task 9 follow-up: send promptly after an identity reset
- Symptom: a message sent right after the reset's `Security` event stayed unsent for 5 to 60 s (seen in `log_hygiene.rs` before 443f88d).
- Cause (core): the status forwarder sent `Security` while the identity task still held the `SyncPause` (the status turns complete before the task ends). A send then ran with the sync stopped; the SDK waits up to 5 s for a pending own-user `/keys/query` (`matrix-sdk-crypto-0.19.1` `store/mod.rs:201-250`) that only the sync sends. The send queue was NOT the cause (it was already on).
- Fix: `IdentityOp::paused` (held by `bootstrap`/`reset` for the whole pause) is waited for by `forward_status` before it reads and sends a status; `IdentityOp::announce` makes the status after such an operation go out even when equal to the last one (a second reset in a row ends in the same status; `a_reset_right_after_login_keeps_the_new_private_keys` caught this). `PauseTarget::settled` makes the restore wait (10 s cap) until the room list is `Running` again. Task 4/5 race protection unchanged (stop before the keys exist, restart after).
- Test: `a_message_sent_right_after_a_reset_is_sent_promptly` in `e2ee_identity.rs` (ignored, `e2ee_serial`): the own "Sent" item is in the list within 10 s of the Security event. Before the fix it stalled 5 s or timed out.
- Ignored runs right after `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, first and repeat: `log_hygiene`, `e2ee_identity` (7), `e2ee_recovery` (7) all green. `log_hygiene.rs` order unchanged.
- Retraction: an earlier reading blamed an SDK timeline race (a stale local echo at "Sending" next to its "Sent" item, about 1 run in 6). It was a bug in the new test: `reset_identity_on` drops the open room's `TimelineDiff` events, so the test's item list drifted from the core's. A debug mirror of the SDK's diffs inside the core showed the SDK list was correct (`Re` x N, then `L-N`, `LeS`, `Re`); the core's mapping is faithful, so no core change is needed for the timeline. The test now runs the reset through a pump (as `log_hygiene.rs` does) and asserts exactly one own item for the message, and that it is "Sent", within 10 s: 30 of 30 runs green with SDK trace logging on (which had made the drift show up). A send right after login was not tested separately.

## Phase 4 / Task 10 notes (security state, banners, screens in the app)
- Step done: new `crates/app/src/security.rs` (pure `SecurityModel`, `banner`, `needs_identity_screen`, `can_finish_setup`, `should_clear_clipboard`, `SecurityMessage`, and the `App` handlers) and `crates/app/src/screens/security.rs` (banner slot, key, restore, identity, reset-warning and password screens). `state.rs` gains `Message::Security`, `App.security` and routes the Phase 4 security events; `main_view` is now `[banner slot, panes, status bar]` inside a fixed `stack` layer (also over the loading screen).
- Choices: the restore field keeps its text after a failed restore (so it can be corrected) and is emptied on success, logout and window close; the copied key is kept in `copied` (zeroizing, redacted) until the 60 s timer has compared the clipboard, so **Done** does not stop the clearing; a closed window cannot clear the clipboard (no task runs after exit). The account-management URL opens only if it is `https` and passes `check_browser_url`, and only while a reset is running; otherwise the reset is cancelled. A reset that fails or is cancelled always says the key backup is gone (Task 4/5 notes). While a reset runs the screen says sync is paused and it may take two minutes; silence is not read as "unchanged". `Security` ends a running reset (the core holds it until sync and the send queue are back). **Use another device** only sends `RequestSelfVerification` (the dialog is Task 11).
- Tests (unit tests through `update` with an `mpsc` sink; the pure functions table-tested; written first, seen failing to compile): banner/identity-screen tables, Done refused until ticked, one command per action, restore trimmed and kept on failure, backup-exists opens restore, reset warning then password then resend, failed/cancelled reset text, https-only approval URL, logout and close drop secrets, sentinel keys never in `Debug`. Tag test `main_view.rs::the_panes_keep_their_place_when_the_banner_and_security_screens_come_and_go`. Ignored bridge test `carol_resets_her_identity_sets_up_recovery_and_logs_out` (bridge.rs, carol).
- APIs confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/*/`: iced_runtime-0.14.0 `clipboard.rs` (`read :29`, `write :49`), iced_widget-0.14.2 `checkbox.rs` (`new :119`, `label :133`, `on_toggle :155`), `stack.rs` (`update :231`, events go top layer first), `helpers.rs` (`opaque :577`), `container.rs` (`danger`, `bordered_box`), `button.rs` (`danger :658`), iced_core `font.rs:27` (`Font::MONOSPACE`), runtime `widget/operation.rs` (`focus`, already used by the composer).
- Review fix: `drop_secrets` (logout, close, disconnect, back to login, a new login) reads the clipboard at once and clears it if it still holds the copied key (`ClipboardFlushed`), so the 60 s timer is not the only path. Tests first in `state.rs`.
- Not covered: `Change key` has no screen (not in the Task 10 list); `ReauthRequired(DeleteDevices)` is ignored until Task 13.
- A first bridge run failed (my bug, not a flake): a `Security` status already in flight ended the reset wait early. Fixed test-first (`a_status_from_before_the_reset_does_not_end_it`): a running reset ends only on a status with the device verified and recovery disabled (`reset_finished`), or on a failure event.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (app lib 461), `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh` all pass. Ignored: `cargo test -p twicecat-app --test bridge -- --ignored` (3 tests, about 5 s) right after `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, then repeated: both green.
- Fix round (phase4.10): on close, exit now waits until the clipboard read has been answered and the clear write issued (`flushes_pending`, `core_closed`, `App::exit_if_flushed`); `ShutdownDeadline` still exits at once. The restore field is emptied on **Back** and whenever `on_status` closes the Restore screen. Tests first (both failed before the code).

## Phase 5 / flaky `notifications::bad_keywords_and_default_modes_are_handled`

- Cause (test race, not account size): the SDK's `NotificationSettings` applies a write to its cache locally, but every push-rules account-data event from sync replaces the whole cache. The echo of the first default-mode write (`MentionsAndKeywords`) can arrive after the second local write (`AllMessages`) and put the old value back; the core publishes a `NotificationSettings` event after each change (the worker and the forwarder), so one or more stale events precede the final one. The test took the first event after the command as the result and read `MentionsAndKeywords`. The core is eventually consistent here and converges when the echo of the last write arrives; nothing in the core was wrong.
- Evidence: with 24 busy loops on the 24-core machine, the old test failed 2 of 8 runs with the reported `left: MentionsAndKeywords, right: AllMessages`; a trace of the events showed `AllMessages, MentionsAndKeywords, MentionsAndKeywords` (failure) and, with the fix, `.., MentionsAndKeywords, MentionsAndKeywords, AllMessages` (two stale events, then the right one). Unloaded runs never failed, which fits "passes on rerun". Slow first sync / room count is ruled out: the test failed on a pruned account with a fast sync, and uses no rooms.
- Fix: the test waits for the specific published value (`Fixture::wait_group_default`) instead of reading the first event; the assertion is as strict as before. 14 loaded runs passed after the change.

## Phase 5 / flaky `rooms::members_have_roles_and_dms_are_reused` (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Cause (a core bug, not the test):**
  - Right after a room is created or joined, `ListRoomMembers` could answer before the sync had brought the room's state. The members come from the server (`/members`), but the roles need the `m.room.create` event (the creators) and the power levels, which only the sync brings.
  - Without them the SDK falls back to default power levels with no creators, so everyone was listed as a plain user at level 0. A debug print showed `creators=None`, no power levels and no room version on the failing call; 0.6 s later the room had them (v10, alice at 100).
  - The app asks for members once, when the member list or room details open, so the creator could be shown without admin rights until the next request. Room details were not affected: `watch_details` resends on every room info change.
- **Evidence:** the test failed 6 of 6 on `main` and 2 of 4 on `feat/call-ui`. alice listed the members as soon as carol had joined, which is the race.
- **Fix:** before listing, the members job waits (at most 10 s, `STATE_WAIT`) until the room's create event is known, using `Room::subscribe_info`.
  - Every room has a create event, and the power levels come in the same sync response (both are in matrix-sdk-ui's `DEFAULT_REQUIRED_STATE`, room_list_service/mod.rs:79).
  - `Room::sync_up` was not used: sliding sync (MSC4186) only ever marks a room's state as partially synced (matrix-sdk-base response_processors/room/msc4186/mod.rs:107), so it would never return.
- **Checks:** the test passed 10 of 10 after the fix; the gate was clean.

## Phase 5 / flaky `notifications::a_keyword_notifies_in_a_mentions_only_room` (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Cause (a core bug, not the test):**
  - The room list's `notification_mode` comes from the SDK's cached user-defined mode. The SDK refreshes that cache after a sync that changes the push rules (matrix-sdk sliding_sync/client.rs:268), but `update_cached_user_defined_notification_mode` raises no `RoomInfoNotableUpdate`. The room list only re-emits an entry on such an update (matrix-sdk-ui room_list_service/room_list.rs:230).
  - So a new mode, set here or on another device, reached the room list only when something else happened in the room.
  - The test usually passed because the room it had just created was still receiving its last updates.
- **Evidence:** the test timed out once in a full all-features run and passed 3 reruns. With the room left quiet for 5 s before the change, it timed out 2 of 2 (30 s, no update ever came).
- **Fix:** a session task, `refresh_room_modes` in `notifications.rs`, follows the push rules (`NotificationSettings::subscribe_to_changes`). For every joined room whose user-defined mode changed, it updates the cached mode and raises a room info update. That update has no reason, so the SDK gives it its catch-all `NONE`, which it documents for updates the room list would otherwise miss. A local change now shows in the room list in about 20 ms.
- **Test:**
  - The test now lets the new room go quiet (a fence, then 3 s) before changing the mode, so it no longer depends on leftover updates.
  - Without the fix it failed 3 of 3; with it, it passed 10 of 10. The gate was clean.
- Also seen in that run: `call_media::remote_voices_are_cleaned_on_the_gpu` (all features) once fell back to the CPU ("GPU too slow"). That was under the full suite's load in a debug build; alone it passed 3 of 3 on CUDA. Not changed here.

## Phase 4 / flaky `bridge::carol_resets_her_identity_sets_up_recovery_and_logs_out` (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** in CI (fresh stack), "no recovery key in 180 s". It failed on `feat/phase6-finish` (`649f10d`, `34c77df`, `3a91b05`) and on `feat/call-ui` (`205f37b`). It never failed locally, where carol already had an identity and a backup.
- **Cause (an app bug):**
  - The app ended its "resetting" wait on the first status that looked like a finished reset (this device verified, no backup; `reset_finished`).
  - On a new account the status *before* a reset is exactly that: login set the identity up and there is no backup. So a status from the login's setup, still on its way when the reset was confirmed, ended the wait while the core's reset still ran.
  - The test's **Set up recovery** then got `busy`, which the test did not look at, so it waited the full 180 s.
  - A user on a new account could hit the same thing: the reset looked done, and the next security action was refused.
- **Reproduced:** with a brand-new account (registered on the local stack as CI's fresh stack gives one) and the whole `bridge` binary pinned to 2 CPUs. It failed 3 of 3, in 6 s, with the notice `busy`. A temporary trace showed `CrossSigning start`, then `Recovery start ok=false`, then `CrossSigning end`.
- **Fix:**
  - The core answers a successful `ResetIdentity` with a new `CoreEvent::IdentityReset`. It is sent after the identity slot is free and after the status that follows the reset: `announce_end` now also waits for the forwarder's round after a success, as it already did after a stop for the password; a failure is still reported at once.
  - The app ends a reset only on that event (`on_reset_done`). A status no longer ends it, and `reset_finished` is gone.
  - The CLI ignores the event.
  - The test now stops at once with the app's notice when the recovery setup fails, instead of waiting 180 s in silence.
- **Tests:**
  - Unit: `a_status_from_before_the_reset_does_not_end_it` now has the new-account status (verified, no backup) and checks that nothing starts until `IdentityReset`. The password-reset test ends on the event too. `a_success_sends_the_status_again_before_it_returns` replaces the old success test of `announce_end`.
  - The new-account scenario passed 11 of 11 after the fix.
  - On the local stack: `e2ee_identity`, `e2ee_recovery`, `e2ee_verification`, `log_hygiene`, the app's `bridge` and `calls` (28 tests) all pass, and the gate is clean (1690 tests).

## Phase 6 / CI: a failed libwebrtc fetch no longer goes on with an empty path (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** `test (macos-latest)` sometimes failed with a panic in `webrtc-sys-build` (lib.rs:245, `parent().unwrap()` on `None`).
- **Cause:**
  - GitHub answered the pinned libwebrtc download with a 504, and `curl --retry 3` (about 7 s of retries) gave up.
  - The workflow ran the fetch inside `echo "LK_CUSTOM_WEBRTC=$(scripts/fetch-libwebrtc.sh)"`. A failed command substitution there does not stop bash, so the step passed and set `LK_CUSTOM_WEBRTC` to an empty string.
  - The empty value overrides `.cargo/config.toml`'s `dev/libwebrtc`, so `webrtc-sys` downloaded libwebrtc itself, without the pinned SHA-256, then panicked on the empty path.
- **Fix:**
  - The three fetch steps in `ci.yml` assign the path first (`dir=$(…)`), as `profile.yml` already did, so a failed fetch fails the step.
  - `fetch-libwebrtc.sh` retries 6 times (about a minute of back-off).
- **Checked:** under CI's `bash -e -o pipefail`, the old form exits 0 with an empty value and the new one exits 1; the workflow still parses; the script still fetches and verifies.

## Phase 5 / flaky `rooms::an_encrypted_room_is_created_invited_and_accepted` (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** it failed once in CI (`6d9c00b`) at `rooms.rs:412`: carol's `RoomJoined` had not arrived yet.
- **Cause (the test):** it waited until carol's room list showed the room as joined, then asserted that the answer to `AcceptInvite` (`RoomJoined`) was already in. The API promises no such order: the sync can bring the joined room before the join request returns, and the core sends `RoomJoined` only then. The app copes with both orders (`select_when_listed`).
- **Fix:** the test waits for both, in either order.
- **Checks:** 6 of 6 runs passed; the gate was clean.

## Phase 6 / flaky `call::cleaning` unit tests on CI (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** `a_bank_that_fails_hands_its_voices_to_the_cpu` failed on `test (ubuntu-26.04)` (`aebc678`): the voices were still `AwaitingModel` where the test expected `Row(0), Row(1)`.
- **Cause (the tests):** the bank and model loads run on blocking threads, while the tests run on paused tokio time. Each simulated 10 ms frame gives the loads about 1 ms of real time, so "100 ms of frames" was about 10 ms of real time. A busy CI runner may not even schedule the load in that window.
- **Fix:** `Rig::frames_until` feeds frames until the awaited result is visible (rows taken and cleaning running, the give-up handled, a new bank loaded), with a 10 s real-time limit. Every wait on a load uses it; the give-up's own timing (`frames(2000)`) is unchanged.
- **Checks:** the 12 cleaning tests pass, also 30 runs pinned to one busy CPU. The old tests did not fail locally under that load either; the CI failure and the 1 ms-per-frame budget are the evidence. The gate was clean.

## Phase 6 / flaky `io::tests::virtual_device_runs_in_real_time` on CI (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** on `test (macos-latest)` (`aebc678`), "7 frames in 200 ms"; the test wanted 10 to 30.
- **Cause (the test):** it counted the frames of a fixed 200 ms sleep. A late device thread on a busy runner makes fewer, and the device skips ahead instead of catching up (`next = now`), so lateness only ever lowers the count.
- **Fix:**
  - It waits (at most 5 s) until 10 frames are out, which shows the device runs.
  - It then checks that the count never exceeds the elapsed real time (one frame per 10 ms, plus 2), which shows it runs in real time and not free-running.
- **Checks:** the test passes, also 30 runs pinned to one busy CPU; the gate was clean.

## Phase 6 / rustls 0.23.45 had four security advisories (fixed 2026-10-14)

- **Found:** cargo-deny in PR #24's lint job (`main` fails it the same way): RUSTSEC-2026-0346 (`Debug` of TLS 1.2/1.3 client session values shows secrets), -0347 (data sent to the outer server after an ECH rejection), -0348 (Brotli certificate decompression can allocate 1 GiB before authentication) and -0349 (an unauthenticated ServerHello can downgrade later connections to classical key exchange). All four are fixed in 0.23.46.
- **Fix:** `cargo update -p rustls` (0.23.45 → 0.23.46, nothing else moved). `crates/core/Cargo.toml` asks for `rustls = "0.23"`, so no pin changed; only the lockfile did.
- **Checks:** the gate was clean (1802 tests, clippy also with `--all-features`, `--locked`); `tls_provider` passes; `login` and `call_signalling` pass on the local stack. cargo-deny is not installed here, so the advisories are checked by CI.

## Phase 6 / joining a call over `wss://` panicked (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** the human joined a call in the app against a real server. The core thread panicked in rustls: "Could not automatically determine the process-level CryptoProvider". The backtrace went through LiveKit's `Room::connect`, then tokio-tungstenite, then `ClientConfig::builder_with_protocol_versions`.
- **Cause:**
  - matrix-sdk turns on rustls' `aws-lc-rs` provider, and LiveKit's reqwest turns on `ring` (`cargo tree -e features -i rustls`). With both built in, rustls will not pick a process default.
  - matrix-sdk's own client passes its provider explicitly, but tokio-tungstenite asks for the process default.
  - The local stack's LiveKit is plain `ws://`, so no test ever reached TLS. Both builds, light and full, were affected.
- **Fix:** `Core::start` installs aws-lc-rs (matrix-sdk's choice) as the process default; one that is already installed stays. `rustls` is now a direct dependency of the core. It was already in the lock at 0.23.45 with that feature, so the lock only gains the dependency entry.
- **Test:** `tests/tls_provider.rs`, a binary of its own so that the process starts without a provider. It checks there is none before `Core::start`, one after, and that `ClientConfig::builder()` (the call that panicked) works. It fails without the fix.
- **Checks:** the gate was clean (1692 tests); `call_signalling` and `login` on the local stack pass.
- **Open (human):** join a call on a real `wss://` server again (added to the checkpoint list).

## Phase 3 / calls cluttered the timeline (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** in a room with calls, the human saw many "X changed the room (org.matrix.msc3401.call.member)" lines, and "X sent something Twicecat cannot show yet" after them.
- **Cause:**
  - The SDK's default timeline filter shows every state event, so each MatrixRTC membership (sent on every join, leave and refresh of a call) became a generic "changed the room" line.
  - The call's `m.rtc.notification`, and the old `m.call.invite`, were mapped to the "unsupported" placeholder (since Phase 3.3, before calls existed).
- **Fix:**
  - Both timelines, the room's and a thread's, use `timeline::event_filter`: the SDK's `default_event_filter`, without `org.matrix.msc3401.call.member` events. The room list's call pill and the call bar show who is in a call.
  - Two new `StateChange` variants: `CallStarted` (`m.rtc.notification`), shown as "X started a call", and `LegacyCall` (`m.call.invite`), shown as "X placed an old-style call Twicecat cannot join".
- **Tests:** `call_memberships_are_hidden_and_the_rest_kept` (a call membership is filtered out; a message and a room name stay); the app's state-text test covers both new lines.
- **Checks:** the gate was clean (1695 tests). On the local stack, `timeline`, `timeline_live`, `timeline_items`, `timeline_actions`, `threads`, `call_signalling`, `call_ringing` and `cli` (41 tests) pass.
- **Open (human):** the room from the screenshot shows "X started a call" lines and no membership lines.

## Phase 6 / a call on a slow link gave up after 5 s per attempt (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** the human's call on their own server did not connect. The log showed "signal connection failed on v1 path: Timeout" on LiveKit's first two attempts, then the join stopped.
- **Cause:**
  - From the human's network, the call server is 690 ms away with 20 % packet loss. `tracepath` shows the delay starting on the transit backbone (hops 9 to 11), not on the home network; the same network reaches 1.1.1.1 in 23 ms. A TLS handshake to the server took 1.4 to 8.6 s.
  - LiveKit 0.9.3 allows 5 s for each attempt to open the signalling websocket (`SIGNAL_CONNECT_TIMEOUT`), and our `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` of 20 s stopped the join during its third attempt.
- **Fix:** `RoomOptions::connect_timeout` is now 15 s (`SIGNAL_TIMEOUT`, as livekit-client, which Element Call uses, allows), and `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` is 45 s, so LiveKit's retries all fit. A good network connects as fast as before.
- **Not fixed by this:** audio over such a link may still be late or choppy (700 ms, lost packets); that is the network.
- **Checks:** the gate was clean (1695 tests). Not tested on a slow link here (it needs a shaped network); added to the checkpoint list.
- **Element's MatrixRTC modes** (the human asked): Twicecat sends and reads the "Compatibility" format (state events, multi-SFU), the one the human's Element uses. It also reads "Legacy" members (oldest-membership SFU) and listens on that SFU. It cannot see "Matrix 2.0" members (MSC4354 sticky `m.rtc.member` events): ruma 0.17 has no such type, as plan decision 10 recorded. A mode setting would only offer one choice, so there is none.

## Phase 6 / the GPU was given up on for a whole call after one hiccup (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** in calls in the app, on the human's RTX 5080 Laptop GPU with nothing else using it, the card said "Running on the CPU: the GPU was too slow". The log showed "missed 3 deadlines in a row" with the last frame at 4.4, 6.2 and 6.9 ms, between 18 s and 100 s into a call.
- **Measured here:** the GPU keeps up with a large margin. `dfn_profile gpu 1500 paced` (new: one frame every 10 ms, as a call) gives a median of 0.7 ms and at most one frame over 4 ms in a row. CLI mic tests on the real microphone stay on CUDA for 30 s (median 0.45–0.66 ms), also while changing settings, with `vkcube` uncapped (GPU 81 % busy), and with all 24 cores busy.
- **Cause:** three late frames in a row (30 ms of a slow GPU: a compositor frame, a clock change, a busy machine) gave the GPU up until the filter was dropped, so for the rest of the call.
- **Fix:**
  - `Deadline` (`dfn::gpu`, shared by the microphone's filter and the bank for other voices) gives up when 10 of the last 100 calls (one second) were late. A GPU late on every frame still gives up after 100 ms.
  - The microphone's filter tries the GPU again (`Retry`): 30 s after giving up, then twice as long after each try that falls behind again, at most 5 min. A try starts the GPU graph from a clean state. A GPU that failed (an error, not slowness) is not tried again. The core's once-a-second check already reports the return to the GPU to the app.
  - The card says "Running on the CPU for now: the GPU fell behind (a game may be using it). It is tried again in a while."; `docs/building.md` explains it.
- **Tests:** `a_few_late_calls_in_a_second_are_tolerated` (the human's pattern), `the_gpu_is_given_up_after_too_many_late_calls_in_a_second`, `a_gpu_given_up_on_is_tried_again_later_and_later`, and on the GPU `a_gpu_that_fell_behind_is_tried_again` (`--features onnxruntime,test-fakes --ignored`; a `test-fakes` hook, `DeepFilter::fall_behind`, makes the try due at once). The audio GPU suite (6) and the core's `gpu_enhancement` (1) pass on CUDA here; the gate was clean (1698 tests; the `dfn::gpu` unit tests run with `--features onnxruntime`, 4 passed).
- **Not done:** the bank for other voices is not tried again after giving up (the CPU filters take over for the call, as before); it only gains the more patient rule.
- **Follow-up investigation (2026-10-13):** with the patient rule the human's calls still fell back every 1 to 5 minutes, with nothing else running. Reproduced with two CLIs in a call on the local stack, using a temporary instrumented build (not committed) that split each GPU call into queueing, waiting and on-GPU time, read the audio thread's schedstat, and sampled `nvidia-smi` every 200 ms.
  - Not the cause: the thread's wake-up (spin, yield and blocking sync gave the same picture, and with spin the thread was on the CPU for the whole slow call), the CPU core (the `twicecat-voice` thread ran on a performance core), another GPU process (two mic tests at once were as fast as one), the compositor (KWin, which drives the HDMI monitor on the NVIDIA GPU, used under 6 % SM), or LiveKit (a mic test without a call was slow too).
  - The cause: the GPU's power state. Our load is about 5 % of the GPU, so the driver leaves it in P8 (180 to 750 MHz, memory at 405 MHz). There a frame takes 1.1 to 2.1 ms on average and up to 15 % of frames take 4 to 15 ms. Whether the GPU sits in P8 or in P4/P5 (1350 to 1800 MHz, 0.7 to 0.95 ms a frame) depends on what else wakes it, so it came and went within minutes. Raising the clocks needs root (`nvidia-smi -lgc`).
  - The CPU, paced like a call on this machine: DeepFilterNet3 takes 0.58 ms a frame on average (p99 1 ms, none over 4 ms), faster than the idle GPU. On a laptop with a fast CPU, the CPU is the better place for one voice; the GPU pays off with a slow or busy CPU (a game) and for many voices at once (the bank).
  - Options left for the human: keep the GPU path as it is (it falls back and retries); or let each GPU call use a whole frame (submit this frame, read it the next), which makes the GPU's speed matter far less but adds 10 ms of latency; pinned host buffers would trim the copies a little but not the long frames.

## Dev / the debug builds filled the disk (fixed 2026-10-12)

- **Symptom:** `target/` reached 259 GB (debug 252 GB: test binaries 146 GB, incremental 80 GB, rlibs 27 GB).
- **Cause:** Cargo keeps every build variant: each mix of features, of `-p` package selections (feature unification differs per selection; `resolver.feature-unification` is still unstable in cargo 1.97.1) and of dependencies gets a full set. Each test binary was about 930 MB (the core, WebRTC and the workspace crates' full debug info), and about 40 of them make one set; there were 5 sets from one day.
- **Fix:**
  - Linux builds keep debug info in split DWARF files (`-C split-debuginfo=unpacked` in `.cargo/config.toml`, x86_64 and aarch64; not in the Cargo.toml profile because Windows MSVC supports only `packed`). One debug build of the tests: 43 GB before, 27 GB after; a test binary 934 MB before, 500 MB after; the build took 143 s instead of 166 s. A panic backtrace still shows file and line, and gdb stops on a breakpoint with the source line.
  - `scripts/trim-target.sh [GB]` removes `target/debug` (`cargo clean --profile dev`) once it is over a limit (80 GB by default); release builds stay.
  - `docs/building.md` gains a "Disk use" section.
- **Cleanup done:** `cargo clean --profile dev` (267 GB); the release build was kept.
- **Left:** the ~40 integration test binaries each link everything; one binary per crate (modules under `tests/`) would save most of the rest, but reorganises the tests and keeps the ones that need a process of their own (`tls_provider`, `log_hygiene`) apart. Not done.
- **Checks:** a clean `cargo test --workspace --no-run` with the new flags; the gate was clean (1698 tests).

## Phase 3 / `timeline_batches_under_16ms` failed on the x86 Linux CI runner (fixed 2026-10-13)

- **Symptom:** PR #16's `test (ubuntu-26.04)` job failed: "1 reset of 10,000: median 16.245726ms is over 16ms". The other five jobs passed, and the branch does not touch `crates/app/src/timeline.rs`.
- **Cause:** the runner is slow for this debug-build timing, not the code. Here the same reset takes a 4 to 5 ms median, also with the whole app suite squeezed onto 2 or 4 cores (`taskset`, `--test-threads`). Passing CI runs do not print the timing, so earlier margins there are unknown; Windows had already needed 50 ms (fd5aafb).
- **Fix:** the 16 ms frame budget stays on developer machines; on Windows or wherever `CI` is set (GitHub Actions sets it) the bound is 50 ms, which still catches an order-of-magnitude regression.
- **Checks:** the test passes with and without `CI=true`; the gate was clean (1698 tests).

## Phase 6 / a screen share's sound replaced the sharer's voice (fixed 2026-10-13)

- **Found:** while planning Phase 9 (ga-lep/twicecat#22, "Before this phase").
- **Symptom (from the code; now reproduced on the local stack):** an Element Call user who shares their screen with sound sends a second audio track (`ScreenshareAudio`) beside the microphone. We played only one of the two. The share's sound was voice-cleaned and counted as the user's mute, and when the share stopped the user went silent.
- **Cause:** `call/media.rs` kept remote audio players by LiveKit identity only, so the second track of the same identity replaced the first, and unsubscribing either one removed the one that was left.
- **Fix:** players are keyed by identity and `Feed` (`Voice` for the microphone or a track that names no source; `Share` for `ScreenshareAudio`). Only the voice is given to `Cleaning`, gives the sender's cleaning claim, and sets the participant's muted flag. Leaving removes every track of the participant. The share plays at the user's volume for now; Phase 9 Task 5 gives it its own.
- **Tests:** `a_screen_share_sound_plays_next_to_the_voice` (`call_media`, local stack). A raw LiveKit client joins as carol with a membership and publishes a 600 Hz microphone and an 800 Hz screen-share sound. Alice hears both (0.30 each, sent 0.3). After the share is unpublished she still hears the voice (0.30) and no share (0.00002). Without the fix the share is not heard (0.0000), so the test fails. A unit test checks the source mapping. `call/media.rs` and the two `call_media` helpers it shares with `a_participant_without_a_membership_is_not_played` (`sfu_room`, `publish_tone`) were reworked for it.
- **Checks:** the gate was clean (1700 tests, clippy also with `--all-features`); `check-app-deps.sh`, `check-core-api.sh`, `check-no-webrtc-download.sh` pass. The full ignored suite on the local stack (`-- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`, models fetched): 154 passed. `call_media` passed 9/9, including the cleaning tests. One failure, `timeline_live::typing_notices_reach_the_other_user` ("carol never saw alice typing", unrelated code), passed when run again (4/4). The binaries `cargo test` had not reached after it (the other crates and `tls_provider`) were then run and pass.
- **Open (human):** an Element Call user sharing a tab with sound: you hear both the voice and the sound, and the voice stays after they stop sharing (checkpoint list).

## Phase 6 / `RUST_LOG=debug` wrote call keys to the log (fixed 2026-10-13)

- **Found:** while planning Phase 9 (ga-lep/twicecat#22, "Before this phase"): webrtc-sdk/webrtc#308 says the native frame cryptor logs key material at `LS_INFO`.
- **Checked on `main`:**
  - Our prebuilt (`webrtc-89d790b`) has the messages: `strings libwebrtc.a` shows `secret `, `raw_key ` and ` derived_key `, the words of the two key-derivation messages (`DeriveHkdfSha256FromSecret`, `DerivePBKDF2KeyFromRawKey`). We use HKDF, so the media key we send over Olm and the AES key derived from it are logged each time a key is set or ratcheted.
  - webrtc-sys turns off libwebrtc's own stderr output, and its sink takes every message from `LS_VERBOSE` up (webrtc-sys 0.3.47 `src/webrtc.cpp:152-164`). libwebrtc passes each message on as `log::debug!(target: "libwebrtc")`, whatever its severity (libwebrtc 0.3.50 `src/native/peer_connection_factory.rs:38-46`). tracing-subscriber's default `tracing-log` feature brings these records into our subscriber.
  - **With the default filters** (`warn,twicecat_core=info`, and the app's with `twicecat_app=info`) the `debug` records are dropped, so nothing was written. **With `RUST_LOG=debug`, `trace` or `libwebrtc=debug`** the keys were written to stderr in full.
- **Fix:** `logging::filter` (now public; `init` uses it) adds `<target>=off` for every target in `call::SECRET_LOG_TARGETS` (`libwebrtc`) after picking the default or `RUST_LOG`. A directive for the same target replaces the one already there (tracing-subscriber 0.3.23 `filter/directive.rs:83-98`), so `RUST_LOG` cannot turn that target back on. libwebrtc's own diagnostics are lost with it, because its sink gives every message the same level and we cannot tell the key messages apart. The target's name lives in `call/e2ee.rs` because `check-core-api.sh` keeps media crate names out of `logging.rs`.
- **Tests:** a unit test (`None`, `trace`, `libwebrtc=trace` and `warn,libwebrtc=debug` all end with `libwebrtc=off` and no other `libwebrtc` directive). `tests/call_log_hygiene.rs` (local stack, its own binary for the global subscriber): an encrypted call between alice and carol, carol leaves (alice sets a new key) and joins again. One layer takes libwebrtc's log as it comes, and must see it. The other uses the core's filter as `RUST_LOG=debug` makes it, and must see no libwebrtc line and no key word. Only counts are printed. On this run the raw layer saw 24,579 libwebrtc lines, 6 of them with key words. The core's filter at `debug` let 0 libwebrtc lines through.
- **Phase 9 Task 1** runs `call_log_hygiene` again after the livekit bump. The raw layer's count of key lines shows whether the new libwebrtc still logs keys.
- **Checks:** the gate was clean (1700 tests, clippy also with `--all-features`); `check-core-api.sh` passes (its comment now lists `filter`); `call_log_hygiene` and `log_hygiene` pass on the local stack.

## Phase 6 / the release-tail test failed on the macOS CI runner (fixed 2026-10-14)

- **Symptom:** PR #23's `test (macos-latest)` job failed in `crates/app/tests/ptt.rs`: `a_release_keeps_transmitting_for_the_delay_then_stops` saw the gate close "3.17472375s after the release" of a 1000 ms tail (the bound is 3 s). The branch does not touch push-to-talk; earlier macOS runs passed.
- **Cause:** the runner was slow, not the code. The gate counts its tail in 10 ms frames (`crates/audio/src/gate.rs`), and the virtual device is paced by a 10 ms sleep that, when it falls behind, starts again from the clock instead of catching up (`AudioIo::start_virtual`). On a starved CPU the 100 frames of the tail take longer than 1 s on the clock, and the test measured the clock. Reproduced here by running the test at `nice 19` on one CPU beside four busy loops: the old test failed 4 of 4 runs ("closed 4.47s", "4.85s"…).
- **Fix:** the test measures in audio. Its silent virtual microphone counts the frames it captures, each level remembers the count when it was seen, and the release tail is checked in frames (still on at least 500 ms of audio after the release, off between 0.7 and 3 s of audio). The no-delay test checks its 500 ms the same way. The waits for the levels allow 30 s and 15 s on the clock instead of 10 s and 5 s, and a failure prints both the audio and the clock time.
- **Checks:** the starved run passed 4 of 4 runs with the new test; the gate was clean (1732 tests, clippy also with `--all-features`); `check-app-deps.sh`, `check-core-api.sh` and `check-no-webrtc-download.sh` pass.

## Phase 6 steps (calls: MatrixRTC, LiveKit SFU, native audio; plan docs/plans/phase6-calls.md)
Order (revised 2026-10-02, plan decision 12; again 2026-10-03 by the human): 1–8, 8b, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 13, then 15 and 13b. Deferred options (audio-only libwebrtc build, pure-Rust WebRTC stack, PulseAudio backend) are recorded in the plan.
- [x] 1. libwebrtc supply chain (pinned SHA-256 fetch script, fail-closed build) and livekit dependency (phase6.1)
- [x] 2. Local SFU stack (LiveKit server, lk-jwt-service, Synapse MatrixRTC config)
- [x] 3. Call API contract and settings persistence
- [x] 4. Audio devices and I/O threads (twicecat-audio, cpal PipeWire)
- [x] 5. Processing chain (sonora AEC3/NS/AGC2, denormal guard, RNNoise, gate, PTT tail)
- [x] 6. Mixer and playout (per-user volume, deafen, AEC render tap, mic test)
- [x] 7. MatrixRTC signalling (transports, JWT, m.call.member, delayed leave)
- [x] 8. LiveKit media (publish, subscribe, reconnection; own SFU)
- [x] 8b. Multi-SFU (subscribe to other members' SFUs; cross-homeserver calls)
- [x] 9. Call E2EE (per-sender keys over to-device)
- [x] 10. Ringing and voice rooms (MSC4075 notifications, MSC4310 declines, call counts, MSC3417 rooms)
- [x] 11. Push-to-talk service (global-hotkey, portal, gamepad, in-window)
- [x] 12. CLI call commands
- [x] 13. DeepFilterNet3 via tract-onnx + tract-pulse, pluggable enhancer
- [x] 13b. GPU speech enhancement, opt-in (part 1: pinned ONNX Runtime, `onnxruntime` feature, accelerator probing and CPU fallback; part 2: stateful DeepFilterNet3 export, CUDA graph loop, `gpu` setting, probe and status; part 3: TensorRT, preferred; part 4: WebGPU plugin provider for other GPUs, with graph capture; batching is Task 16, step 16 below; Maxine not done)
- [x] 14. Own PipeWire client on Linux (communication role, latency, registry events, system-mix echo reference)
- [ ] 15. Steam Frame performance (CPU budget per mode, Frame defaults, rtkit priority) — part 1 done: profiling harness and x86_64 per-stage numbers; part 3: real-time priority on Linux, wake-ups; part 4: Windows and macOS priority, lateness in `AudioStats`, Opus cost; open: aarch64 numbers and Frame defaults
- [x] 16. Cleaning other people's voices, opt-in (added 2026-10-03; CPU filters, quiet voices rest, skip voices their clients clean, batched GPU bank; the core's switch from a failing bank to the CPU is covered by tests, phase6-finish Task 1). Human items: by ear on the CPU and the GPU, Element Call's flags, the Frame's limit (Phase 6 human checkpoint)

### Step 15: one command for the call path (phase6-finish Task 2, phase6.15)
- **Built:** `scripts/profile-call-path.sh` and a `--tsv` flag on `voice_profile` (also `--voices N`) and `opus_profile`; each example has a unit test of its TSV line (`[[example]] test = true` in the two manifests, so `cargo test --workspace` runs them). Call path = chain row + (engine with N voices mixed less engine with none) + Opus encode at 64 kb/s complexity 9 + N decodes, medians and p99s of the parts added (an upper bound); two scenarios per mode, talking (voice in noise) and silent (digital silence). Opus rows are the unflushed ones (WebRTC's encoder thread); the chain runs with denormals flushed, as the processing thread does.
- **One run, this x86_64 machine** (AMD Ryzen 7 7800X3D, 8 cores/16 threads, no E-cores, so no P/E comparison; `--voices 4 --frames 1000`, release; median / p99 µs per 10 ms, share of the 500 µs budget; none OVER):

| Mode | Talking, unpinned | Silent, unpinned | Talking, CPU 2 | Silent, CPU 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| off | 66.4 / 81.2 (13 % / 16 %) | 31.1 / 33.9 | 69.3 / 84.8 | 34.8 / 45.8 |
| WebRTC NS | 80.7 / 99.2 (16 % / 20 %) | 45.4 / 51.9 | 83.7 / 105.8 | 49.2 / 66.8 |
| RNNoise | 98.2 / 125.1 (20 % / 25 %) | 62.9 / 77.8 | 100.3 / 121.3 | 65.8 / 82.3 |
| DeepFilterNet3 (CPU) | 318.8 / 440.6 (64 % / 88 %) | 283.5 / 393.3 | 328.1 / 385.4 | 293.6 / 346.4 |

- DeepFilterNet3's p99 share depends on the run length (88 % at 1000 frames, 96 % at 200, 102.7 % at 100), so "none OVER" above is not stable across runs; use `--frames 1000` or more for a verdict.
- **Not verified:** shellcheck was not installed when the script was first written (`bash -n` only); a later pass ran it through `pipx run --spec shellcheck-py shellcheck` with no findings; the E-core numbers and aarch64 numbers (Task 3) need other hardware; the Frame's defaults stay a human decision.

### Step 15: the silence options for the Opus encoder (phase6-finish Task 4, phase6.15)
- **Built:** `opus_profile` rows for digital silence, a seeded dither at -110 and -90 dBFS RMS (`dither`: sparse ±1 LSB at one sample in 94, and ±2 LSB at one in 4; the unit tests check the RMS within 0.5 dB and the peak, and that it repeats), and "no frames (not possible with LiveKit's source)" (encoder not called: 0 µs, 0 packets, 0 kb/s by construction; the row's label says so, still 7 TSV columns). Every row now also gives kb/s of payload on the wire (packets sent only, RTP/SRTP headers extra); `--tsv` has it as a seventh column (columns 3 and 4, which `profile-call-path.sh` reads, are unchanged). No change to the call path.
- **"Stopping frames while silent" answered from the source** (webrtc-sys 0.3.47, libwebrtc 0.3.50, read in the registry): not calling `NativeAudioSource::capture_frame` does not stop the encoder.
  - Our source is made with a 50 ms queue (`crates/core/src/call/media.rs:45,259-269`); `NativeAudioSource::new` passes it on (`libwebrtc-0.3.50/src/native/audio_source.rs:49-61`), and a non-zero queue starts a 10 ms repeating task (`webrtc-sys-0.3.47/src/audio_track.cpp:143-166`, `RepeatingTaskHandle::Start` ... `TimeDelta::Millis(10)` at :186).
  - Each tick sends the queued 10 ms to every sink, or else a 10 ms buffer of zeros: "Always provide a 10ms frame to avoid playout underruns" (`audio_track.cpp:164-178`; `silence_buffer_` is zeroed at :151).
  - `capture_frame` only appends to the queue (`audio_track.cpp:194-218`). So without calls the sinks (the send stream) still get digital silence every 10 ms, and the encoder runs on it: "no frames" is the same as the digital silence row, not the 0 µs row. Only the queue-0 mode (`audio_track.cpp:143-147`, `capture_frame` fast path at :221-226) would feed nothing, and we do not use it. What the send stream does with a source that delivers nothing is in the prebuilt libwebrtc C++ (not in the registry), so not read; not tested. Unpublishing or muting the track at the SFU is a different, call-path change and not measured.
- **Measured, this machine** (AMD Ryzen 7 7800X3D, no E-cores, so **P-core numbers only** (`taskset -c 2`, release, 10 s); the E-core run is a human item, below). µs of encode per 10 ms, % of packets sent (not DTX), payload kb/s. Flushing denormals changed no row by more than 3 µs on this CPU (Zen 4 has no denormal penalty), so one value per row; flushed values are in the full output.

| Signal | 64 kb/s c9 | 32 kb/s c5 |
|---|---|---|
| digital silence | 9.2 µs, 5 %, 0.1 kb/s | 32.3 µs, 5 %, 0.7 kb/s |
| dither -110 dBFS | 78.2 µs, 100 %, 30.6 kb/s | 33.1 µs, 5 %, 0.7 kb/s |
| dither -90 dBFS | 80.4 µs, 100 %, 35.7 kb/s | 35.7 µs, 5 %, 0.9 kb/s |
| no frames (encoder not called; not possible in LiveKit's source) | 0, 0 %, 0 | 0, 0 %, 0 |
| (voice in noise, for scale) | 27.4 µs, 100 %, 64.4 kb/s | 45.2 µs, 100 %, 28.7 kb/s |

- **Does a dither defeat DTX? It depends on the complexity.** At complexity 9 (64 kb/s, and 32 kb/s c9: 77 to 79 µs, 100 %, 14 to 17.5 kb/s) both dither levels, even sparse ±1 LSB at -110 dBFS, keep DTX from stopping: every packet is sent, the cost is 8.5 times digital silence, and the wire carries 31 to 36 kb/s instead of 0.1. At 32 kb/s complexity 5 neither level defeats DTX (5 % sent, the same as digital silence; 33 to 36 µs against 32). So the step 15 note is right for the desktop default (c9) and wrong for c5. A dither therefore does not help on this CPU at c9; whether it helps against the E-core denormal penalty (7x on silence there) is not known until the E-core run.
- **Not verified:** E-core numbers (this machine has none); aarch64 and the Frame (Task 3's job and the device); how the send stream treats a source that stops delivering.

### Step 16 closed for its agent part (phase6-finish Task 1)
- **Built:** `Loaders` (bank and CPU-model loaders) in `crates/core/src/call/cleaning/mod.rs`, real ones by default, fakes in `crates/core/src/call/cleaning/tests.rs`; `Cleaning::new` and its callers are unchanged. Four tests drive a real `VoiceEngine` with two voices and a fake `BatchCleaner`: a failing bank ends in `GaveUp(CpuAfterGpuError)` with no voice on a row, a CPU model load asked for and no voice shown `Cleaned` with its flag down; a bank that set `CpuAfterGpuTooSlow` first ends in `GaveUp(CpuAfterGpuTooSlow)`; turning `gpu` off and on loads a second bank; a leaving voice frees its row (the next gets row 0). The ignored test `the_cpu_filters_take_over_after_a_give_up` (needs `scripts/fetch-models.sh`; the CI `integration` job fetches the models first) ends with both voices `Cleaned` and `Running` and the backend slot at `Some(CpuAfterGpuError)`.
- **No defect found** in the give-up path. One change for testability: `Entry::handed_over` and the start grace use `tokio::time::Instant` (the same clock as `std` outside tests) so paused time drives the grace.
- **API:** `twicecat_audio::DeepFilterStatus::new` and `set` are `pub` only with the `test-fakes` feature of `twicecat-audio` (enabled by `twicecat-core` under `[dev-dependencies]` only, so a fake bank can report "too slow"); normal builds keep them `pub(crate)` (`new_internal`, `set_internal`), so this is not a permanent audio API addition; the core's public API is unchanged except the rustdoc of `CleaningAnnouncement`, which now states the privacy of the attribute (seen by the call and the SFU's operator).
- **Mutations** (each reverted): removing the `gave_up` branch in `watch_failures` fails the give-up and too-slow tests and the ignored CPU test; not excluding `Gpu::GaveUp` from `use_gpu` fails the give-up test and the ignored CPU test; not freeing `rows[row]` in `remove` fails the row test; not resetting `gpu` to `Absent` when the setting goes off fails the off-and-on test; mapping the status to the error case only fails the too-slow test.
- **Checks:** `cargo fmt --all`; clippy with and without `--all-features`, zero warnings; `cargo test --workspace` (477 passed in twicecat-core, 0 failed); `cargo test -p twicecat-core --lib -- --ignored call::cleaning` 1 passed; `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`), `check-core-api.sh`, `check-no-webrtc-download.sh` pass. Built with `CC=clang-22 CXX=clang++-22`.

## Phase 6 summary (compacted 2026-10-04 for review)
The per-task notes (checks run, every API read with its source line, every measurement) were compacted here on 2026-10-04; the full text is in git history (`git show fd452df..HEAD -- PROGRESS.md`, or `PROGRESS.md` at the branch's commits). The plan (`docs/plans/phase6-calls.md`) keeps the design and the measurements per task.

### What each task built
| Task | Built | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | libwebrtc fetched by `scripts/fetch-libwebrtc.sh` (pinned SHA-256, fails closed; webrtc-sys never downloads: `dev/libwebrtc` always exists); its weak `pw_*` stubs renamed so they cannot shadow libpipewire; livekit =0.9.3 | `scripts/`, `.cargo/config.toml` |
| 2 | Local SFU stack: two LiveKit servers and two lk-jwt-services (multi-SFU tests), Synapse with MatrixRTC transports and delayed events | `docker-compose.yml`, `dev/` |
| 3 | Call and audio API (SDK-free), `call_settings.json` (versioned, atomic, 0600) | `core/src/api/{call,audio}.rs`, `core/src/call_settings.rs` |
| 4, 14 | Device I/O: our own PipeWire client on Linux (communication role, 10 ms quanta, hot-plug from registry events, the system mix as echo reference), cpal elsewhere; resampling to 48 kHz 10 ms frames; lock-free rings | `audio/src/{io,pw,pipeline,resample,devices}` |
| 5 | Processing chain: high-pass, AEC3, WebRTC NS / RNNoise / a plugged enhancer, AGC2, gain, gate or push-to-talk, limiter (sonora, nnnoiseless); denormal guard | `audio/src/{chain,gate,denormals}.rs` |
| 6 | Voice engine: per-source volume, deafen, mic-test loopback, the echo reference is exactly what is played | `audio/src/voice/` |
| 7 | MatrixRTC signalling as Element Call's compatibility mode: transports (MSC4143), JWT, `m.call.member`, delayed leave (MSC4140) restarted every 4 s, refresh before expiry, rejoin when the membership vanishes | `core/src/call/{membership,own_membership,jwt}.rs` |
| 8, 8b | LiveKit media: publish the processed microphone, play members' tracks; one connection per SFU (other homeservers' SFUs subscribe-only), only active members played; call audio on its own thread | `core/src/call/{media,sfus,participants}.rs` |
| 9 | Call E2EE as Element Call (per-sender keys over Olm to-device, rotation on leave, keys only from verified Olm senders) | `core/src/call/e2ee.rs` |
| 10 | Ringing and voice rooms (MSC4075 notifications, MSC4310 declines, call counts, MSC3417 rooms) | `core/src/call/{ringing,watcher}.rs` |
| 11 | Push-to-talk service: global hotkey (X11, Windows, macOS), the GlobalShortcuts portal (Wayland), gamepad, in-window | `crates/ptt` |
| 12 | `twicecat-cli` call and audio commands | `core/src/bin/twicecat-cli/calls/` |
| 13 | DeepFilterNet3 on tract (our STFT, features and deep filter around the pulsed networks), pinned models | `audio/src/dfn/` |
| 13b | Opt-in GPU DeepFilterNet3 through ONNX Runtime (feature `onnxruntime` / `gpu-enhancement`): pinned runtime, stateful export, TensorRT then CUDA (state on the GPU, captured CUDA graph), WebGPU plugin for other GPUs; CPU fallback on error or missed deadlines | `audio/src/onnx/`, `audio/src/dfn/{filter,gpu,stateful}.rs`, `core/src/gpu_enhancement.rs` |
| 15 | Profiling harness, x86-64-v3 builds with a CPUID check, real-time priority (PipeWire's module on Linux, MMCSS / time-constraint elsewhere), lateness and xruns in `AudioStats`, Opus cost measured | `audio/examples/voice_profile.rs`, `core/src/cpu_level.rs`, `core/examples/opus_profile.rs` |
| 16 | Cleaning other people's voices, opt-in: per-voice CPU filters for the talkers (quiet voices rest, loudest first, at most 4 at once, 16 per call); skip voices cleaned at their source (our LiveKit attribute `twicecat.voice_cleaning`, LiveKit track flags); one batched GPU call per frame for all voices | `core/src/call/cleaning/`, `audio/src/voice/sources.rs`, `audio/src/dfn/bank.rs` |

### Measurements that matter (i9-14900K, release, per 10 ms frame)
| Path | P-core | E-core |
|---|---|---|
| Default chain (HPF, AEC3, RNNoise, AGC2) | 80 µs | 158 µs |
| Mixing | 0.8 µs per voice | |
| DeepFilterNet3 on the CPU (microphone) | 270 µs | 517 µs |
| Engine, 4 voices talking, all cleaned on the CPU | 1.18 ms | 2.25 ms |
| Engine, 16 voices cleaned in the GPU bank (TensorRT, RTX 5090) | 290 µs | 470 µs |
| DeepFilterNet3 on the GPU, one voice: TensorRT / CUDA / WebGPU | 0.13 / 0.35 / 0.59 ms (37 / 58 / 420 µs of CPU) | |
| Opus encode, 64 kb/s complexity 9: voice / room noise / digital silence | 20.6 / 68 / 30.6 µs | silence 210 µs |
| Wake latency under full CPU load: real-time / normal priority | worst 0.1 ms / 3.2–4.4 ms | |

Quality: DeepFilterNet3 lifts speech in noise from 5.1 to 14.6 dB SNR (RNNoise 8.7 dB) on DeepFilterNet's sample clips; the GPU paths agree with the CPU within 63 dB or better; AEC3 removes 20 dB or more of a synthetic echo.

## Phase 6 / Task 5 notes
- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings` (a first run failed on a `collapsible_if` in gate.rs, fixed); `cargo test --workspace --locked` (twicecat-audio 29 passed, 2 ignored; workspace total 171 passed, 17 ignored); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check` (sonora's 7 crates and nnnoiseless: BSD-3-Clause, deny.toml unchanged); ignored suite 14/14.

Mutation checks: with AEC disabled the AEC test measures 0.0 dB and fails; with RNNoise bypassed the NS test measures 0.0 dB and fails. Both reverted.
- **sonora #34 reproduced, and the guard confirmed** (`cargo test --release -p twicecat-audio -- --ignored timing`, x86_64): after 3 s of far-end speech, a silent far end costs 482.7 µs mean / 613.6 µs worst per 10 ms frame without the denormal guard, and 88.2 µs / 120.0 µs with it (full default chain: HPF, AEC3, RNNoise, AGC2). Only silence that follows speech shows it: silence from the start was 96 µs vs 91 µs, because the filter states have to decay into denormals first.
- APIs used (registry src/): sonora `AudioProcessing::builder().config().capture_config().render_config().build()` (src/audio_processing.rs:311-365), `apply_config` (:452), `process_capture_f32(&[&[f32]], &mut [&mut [f32]])` and `process_render_f32` (:615, :643), `statistics() -> &AudioProcessingStats { echo_return_loss_enhancement, residual_echo_likelihood, delay_ms, .. }` (:537; src/stats.rs:11-38), `StreamConfig::new(rate, channels)`, `Config { high_pass_filter, echo_canceller, noise_suppression, gain_controller2, .. }` and the `EchoCanceller`, `HighPassFilter`, `NoiseSuppression { level: NoiseSuppressionLevel }`, `GainController2 { adaptive_digital: Option<AdaptiveDigital>, .. }` defaults (src/config.rs:32-290). nnnoiseless `DenoiseState::new() -> Box<DenoiseState<'static>>`, `process_frame(&mut [f32], &[f32]) -> f32` (VAD probability; samples in i16 scale; src/denoise.rs:53, :95), `default-features = false` (the defaults pull a CLI's deps). Inline asm `stmxcsr`/`ldmxcsr` and `mrs`/`msr fpcr` (std::arch::asm).
- `unsafe`: `crates/audio/src/denormals.rs` (decision 7), with SAFETY comments and a test that denormals flush on the calling thread and stay IEEE on others; since Task 13b the CUDA loop's calls into the CUDA runtime (`crates/audio/src/onnx/stateful_loop.rs`, feature `onnxruntime`), and since its part 4 one call of ONNX Runtime's `CopyTensors` for the WebGPU loop (same file); since Task 15 the x86-64-v3 CPU check (`crates/core/src/cpu_level.rs`, assembly) and the processing thread made through PipeWire's real-time module (`crates/audio/src/pw/thread.rs`: its thread-utils calls, the exported `pw_thread_utils_create`/`join` and `pthread_self`, declared there because the bindings lack them); since Task 15 part 4 the calls into libwebrtc's Opus in the `opus_profile` example (`crates/core/examples/opus_profile.rs`, a measuring tool, not shipped). Each has SAFETY comments. Rule (human, 2026-10-03): `unsafe` only where needed, for low-level work or a hard dependency; the first three approved, the PipeWire one under that rule. aarch64 is CI-compiled; the arm64 test job runs that test.
