It runs on the processing thread, not in device callbacks, so the rings absorb an occasional slow frame, but steady state is not allocation-free until a sonora release fixes it (bump asked first). Real-time thread priority for the processing thread is not set yet. Gate thresholds and AGC behaviour need checking by ear (human list).
- New human items: none beyond the list (audio quality by ear, Steam Frame CPU per NS mode).

### `unsafe` (rule: only where needed, SAFETY comments on each)
`audio/src/denormals.rs` (FTZ/DAZ, decision 7); `audio/src/wasapi/` (Windows' MMDevice API and WASAPI over COM: the communications defaults, endpoint notifications, our own streams and their buffers; 2026-10-05, under the rule); `audio/src/onnx/nvidia.rs` on Windows (loading NVIDIA's DLLs with `LOAD_WITH_ALTERED_SEARCH_PATH`); `audio/src/pw/thread.rs` (PipeWire's thread utilities, `pthread_self`); `audio/src/onnx/mod.rs` (`ComputeStream`, the providers' compute stream); `audio/src/onnx/stateful_loop/cuda.rs` (the CUDA runtime: streams, copies, fills, the thread's capture mode); `audio/src/onnx/stateful_loop/webgpu.rs` (ONNX Runtime's `CopyTensors`); `core/src/cpu_level.rs` (CPUID check in assembly, run before Rust code); `core/examples/opus_profile.rs` (libwebrtc's Opus), `audio/examples/voice_profile.rs` (a counting allocator) and `audio/examples/dfn_profile.rs` on Windows (`GetProcessTimes`; 2026-10-06), measuring tools, not shipped. All approved (2026-10-03, 2026-10-04).

### Review of the branch (2026-10-04)
- **Layout:** large files split by concern, one commit's worth each, folded into the commits that wrote them:
  - audio: `voice/` (`mod`, `control`, `sources`, `tests`), `dfn/` (`signal`, `filter`, `bank`, `gpu`), `onnx/` (`nvidia`, `webgpu`, `stateful_loop/`), `io/` (`cpal`), `enhancer.rs` (the plug-in traits), `paths.rs` (the cache directory, once instead of five copies);
  - core: `call/` (`own_membership`, `participants`, `sfus`, `cleaning/` with its `policy`), `engine/calls.rs`, `api/audio.rs`;
  - CLI: `calls/` (`mod`, `parse`, `show`).
  - Public API unchanged (`scripts/check-core-api.sh`).
  - Not split, as they are older phases' code (rule 8, for the human): `core/src/engine/mod.rs` (login and sync, 1,442 lines) and `app/src/state.rs`.
- **Single source of truth:**
  - The cleaning wire values are constants.
  - One lock helper for the voice control.
  - One place builds call audio.
  - The GPU parts are looked up once.
  - The CPU and GPU DeepFilterNet paths share their signal code (`NetIo`).
- **Dead code removed:**
  - `DeepFilter::local_snr_db`.
  - `OnnxLoop::rows`.
  - The `latency_samples` and `rows` methods of the `Enhancer` and `BatchCleaner` traits, which nothing read.
  - The redundant test that the chain is `Send`, which the compiler already checks through `FrameProcessor`.
- **Performance** (`voice_profile` before and after, same core):
  - Timing is unchanged.
  - DeepFilterNet3's CPU output is byte-identical.
  - Allocations: the GPU bank with 16 voices went from 103.5 to 55.5 per frame (network buffers reused per voice), and a CPU filter from 1028 to 1023.
  - No bottleneck in our code. What remains is upstream or the human's call:
    - Opus on digital silence on E-cores;
    - sonora's allocations and AGC2's VAD;
    - tract's allocations against shipping ONNX Runtime's CPU path.
- **Coverage** (llvm-cov, every suite but `keyring` and `hardware_*`, GPU suites included): 88.3% of lines in the workspace, 87.6% in the files this branch changed; by crate, core 89.7%, audio 88.5%, app 88.4%, ptt 56.8%. The CLI's call commands went from 75% to 92%, `gpu_enhancement.rs` from 81% to 92%.
  - Under 80%, and why:
    - `ptt` (hotkey, portal, gamepad: a desktop session or a device; human checks);
    - `audio/src/io/cpal.rs` and `devices.rs` (cpal, used off Linux);
    - GPU and server failure branches (`dfn/bank.rs`, `dfn/filter.rs`, `onnx/stateful_loop/mod.rs`, `call/sfus.rs`, `call/own_membership.rs`, `engine/calls.rs`: reconnection and lost SFUs);
    - Phase 1/2 files (`app` screens, `secrets.rs`).
  - Added:
    - the GPU probe from a fresh start (`gpu_enhancement`, never run before);
    - every CLI settings command and the per-person cleaning, mic test and call events.

### How to run the checks
- Gate: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and again with `--all-features`; `cargo test --workspace`; `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `scripts/check-commits.sh main`.
- Local stack: `scripts/stack.sh up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh` (addresses and ports: `.env.test`, `.env.local`, the environment; plan Task 2), then `cargo test --workspace -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_` (the agent never runs `keyring` or `hardware_*`).
- GPU (Task 13b parts installed): `cargo test --release -p twicecat-audio --features onnxruntime --test onnxruntime --test deepfilter -- --ignored` (exits cleanly: a crash at exit is a regression) and `cargo test -p twicecat-core --features gpu-enhancement --test call_media --test gpu_enhancement -- --ignored`.
- Real speech: `TWICECAT_SPEECH_WAV` and `TWICECAT_NOISE_WAV` (DeepFilterNet's `assets/clean_freesound_33711.wav` and `noise_freesound_2530.wav` work) for `on_real_speech_deep_filter_improves_the_snr_more_than_rnnoise`, which skips without them.
- Cross-checks: `CC_x86_64_pc_windows_msvc=clang-cl AR_x86_64_pc_windows_msvc=llvm-lib cargo clippy -p twicecat-audio --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -- -D warnings`; `CC_aarch64_apple_darwin=clang AR_aarch64_apple_darwin=llvm-ar cargo clippy -p twicecat-audio --all-targets --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- -D warnings`.
- Coverage: `rustup component add llvm-tools --toolchain 1.97.1` (the system's LLVM 23 cannot read rustc 1.97's profiles), then build and run the tests with `RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3 -C instrument-coverage" LLVM_PROFILE_FILE=<dir>/%p-%m.profraw CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<another dir>` and `--all-features` (the unit tests, then the ignored suites as above), `llvm-profdata merge -sparse`, `llvm-cov report` over the test binaries.
- Profiles: `cargo run --release -p twicecat-audio [--features onnxruntime] --example voice_profile` (pin with `taskset -c <cpu>`; `VOICE_PROFILE_TRACE=n` names who allocates), `--example dfn_profile`, `cargo run --release -p twicecat-core --example opus_profile`. Both take `--tsv` (one tab-separated line per row: name, mean, median, p99, worst, allocations or packets sent).
- Call path against the Frame budget (phase6-finish Task 2): `scripts/profile-call-path.sh [--voices N] [--frames N] [--cpu C]` builds both examples in release and prints, per noise-suppression mode, the call path per 10 ms (chain + mixing N voices + Opus encode + N decodes) at the median and p99 as a share of the 500 µs budget, `OVER` when exceeded; it also writes `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`. Downloads nothing; DeepFilterNet3 only when `scripts/fetch-models.sh` has been run. Pin with `--cpu C` (run it on an E-core too).

### Windows, native (2026-10-05)
First build and gate on Windows itself (Windows 11 26300, i9-14900K, RTX 5090; MSVC 14.44 Build Tools, Git Bash; before this, Windows was only cross-checked with clippy from Linux).
- **Gate:** `cargo fmt --all --check`, both clippy runs (`--all-features` too) and `cargo test --workspace`: 492 passed, 0 failed, 47 ignored (with `dev/libwebrtc` linked by the script, no `LK_CUSTOM_WEBRTC`); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`), `check-core-api.sh`, `check-no-webrtc-download.sh` pass. Not run here: `cargo deny` (not installed), `keyring`, `hardware_*`.
- **Homeserver suites on Windows** (`cargo test -p twicecat-core -p twicecat-app -j 6 --no-fail-fast -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`): 37 passed, 0 failed, with every stack port moved through `.env.local` (18008, 18080, 1788x, 18081, 1789x). The stack runs in Docker Engine (Ubuntu's `docker.io` and `docker-compose-v2`) in an Ubuntu 26.04 WSL distribution with WSL's mirrored networking (`networkingMode=mirrored` in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`), so 127.0.0.1 is shared with Windows, UDP included (LiveKit's ICE port; WSL's default NAT forwards only TCP). WSL stops an idle distribution, Docker with it: keep a session open while testing (`wsl -d Ubuntu-26.04 -- sleep infinity`). WSL 3.0's own containers (`wslc`) were looked at: Docker-like, but no compose, so the stack would need a second definition.
- **Fixed** (folded into the commits that wrote the code): `twicecat-ptt`'s `APP_ID` is used only by the Wayland portal and failed `-D warnings` off Linux (Task 11; the Windows and macOS CI jobs would have failed too); `scripts/fetch-libwebrtc.sh` now links `dev/libwebrtc` on Windows as well, directories as junctions (no privileges needed) and the top-level files copied, so `LK_CUSTOM_WEBRTC` no longer has to be exported there (Task 1).
- **Fixed** (Windows only, found by the homeserver suites): a failed login and a logout left the session's store directory behind. Windows cannot delete an open file, and the SDK's four SQLite stores close 25 to 50 ms after the client is dropped; removal now retries while a file is in use, at most 2 s (`paths::remove_dir_all_when_released`). And the cache directory for models and runtimes came only from `HOME`, which a Twicecat started outside Git Bash does not have: it now uses the platform's home directory (the user profile on Windows), the same `~/.cache/twicecat` the fetch scripts write (folded into Task 16 part 3, where `audio/src/paths.rs` was written).
- **Built:** the local stack's address and ports are settings (`.env.test`, `.env.local`, the environment; `scripts/stack.sh`; plan Task 2), after NVIDIA Broadcast was found holding 127.0.0.1:8080 here (this machine's `.env.local` moves lk-jwt-service to 8090).
- **Built:** the system-mix echo reference (`/aec system`) on Windows through WASAPI loopback, with a 30 ms bound on the queued reference (the loopback runs on the output device's clock); the reference now also reaches the processor without a microphone (plan Task 14, "Windows").
- **Seen on real devices** (output only, no microphone opened): the Task 4 output test passes; the loopback test hears its −46 dBFS tone 6 dB down through Elgato Wave Link's virtual device; the processing thread got MMCSS (`Realtime::GrantedBy("MMCSS Audio task")`), the Windows half of the Task 15 part 4 human item (by ear under load still open).
- **Finding:** `cargo test --workspace` at full parallelism (32 threads) ran out of Windows' commit limit (64 GB RAM, 15 GB page file: "The paging file is too small", E0786) linking the test binaries, each with libwebrtc and matrix-sdk; `-j 6` passes. `scripts/check-core-api.sh` needs a UTF-8 locale for `grep -P` (`LC_ALL=C.UTF-8`; Git Bash's own terminal sets one).
- **Finding:** a clone made with Git for Windows' default `core.autocrlf=true` checks the scripts out with CRLF; this one uses `core.autocrlf=false`. A `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`) would settle it for every Windows clone (for the human: it touches every file's checkout, not its content).
- **Finding:** the three remote-cleaning call tests need `scripts/fetch-models.sh` first (they time out waiting for the "cleaned" state without the models); with them, carol's tone falls from 0.285 to 0.00014 cleaned. `dev/synapse/start.sh` line 12 has an old shellcheck warning (SC3028, `UID` in POSIX sh), left as it is.
- **Built (devices):** streams reopen in place when their device goes away or the default they follow changes (WASAPI never moves one: before, a call lost its sound until the next one); the default devices are the *communications* ones; hot-plug comes from the MMDevice API's notifications (30 s poll as a fallback); WASAPI underruns and overruns count as xruns instead of reaching the user as "stream failed" (plan Task 4, "Windows devices"). New direct dependencies `windows` and `windows-core` =0.62.2, the versions cpal already uses (no new crates in the lock; human choice 2026-10-05). Checked on this machine: device tests 3/3 (output, loopback, reopen), the probe lists the communications defaults and plays on them with MMCSS.
- **Built (WASAPI client):** on Windows the streams are our own WASAPI client's, each marked as a call (`AudioCategory_Communications`), with Windows converting to 48 kHz mono; cpal stays for listing devices and as a fallback (`TWICECAT_AUDIO_BACKEND=cpal` forces it). The loopback and reopening device tests run on our streams (plan Task 4, "Our own WASAPI client").
- **Built (GPU on Windows):** the CUDA, cuDNN and TensorRT fetch scripts take NVIDIA's `win_amd64` wheels (same versions, published digests pinned beside the Linux ones; human request 2026-10-05); the WebGPU fetch also unpacks the DirectX shader compiler its Direct3D 12 backend needs (it did not start without); NVIDIA's DLLs are preloaded on Windows; the model export runs on Windows and gives the pinned SHA-256. All three backends run here: TensorRT 259–269 µs per frame, CUDA 567–617 µs, WebGPU 1.54 ms (Vulkan on Linux: 0.59 ms), all 88–133 dB from the CPU's output (plan Task 13b, "Windows").
- **Built (DirectML, 2026-10-06, human request):** Microsoft publishes no DirectML build of ONNX Runtime after 1.24.4, so `scripts/build-onnxruntime-directml.sh` builds the pinned 1.28.3 tag (commit checked) with DirectML as Microsoft's own build does, checks the DirectML package it restores against the pinned NuGet package (1.15.4), and installs it as the `win-x64-directml` flavour, loaded after the CUDA builds and before the CPU one (15 minutes here). On the RTX 5090 DirectML runs DeepFilterNet3 132.1 dB from the CPU's output (WebGPU 133.3 dB) at 0.30 ms per frame for 0.11 ms of CPU (`dfn_profile`; WebGPU over Direct3D 12: 0.93 ms for 0.85 ms, CUDA 0.61 ms for 0.11 ms, TensorRT 0.22 ms for 0.07 ms); a 16-row bank costs the same on both. So DirectML is now tried before WebGPU (human, 2026-10-06: the most optimised first, at equal quality); the dependency is approved. The runtime loads its own DirectML.dll, not the older one in System32 (plan Task 13b, "Windows").
- **Review before production (2026-10-06, human request; two independent reviews of the branch's diff, fixes folded into the commits they belong to):** the echo reference is best effort (a device that cannot be looped back used to stop playback with it); a displaced stream (fallen back, or with no device) looks for a way back every 5 s on its own (macOS and Linux without PipeWire had no trigger, nor Windows failures without a notification), and its lost message carries the reason; falling back is reported once, not on each reopening; a default change no longer reopens a stream already on the new default; the DirectML build installs the checked DirectML package before the build instead of comparing three files after it; NVIDIA's DLL directory is made absolute before `LOAD_WITH_ALTERED_SEARCH_PATH`; `scripts/stack-env.sh` trims like the Rust tests, `scripts/stack.sh` warns for any address but 127.0.0.1 (and for ::1, untested); stale comments and docs. Gate 502/0/48; device tests 3/3 on our streams; GPU audio suites 9/9 on the DirectML build; Linux clippy in WSL.
- **Open (Windows):** by ear and by hand (below); DirectML on AMD and Intel GPUs.

## Phase 6 known limitations
- Denormal guard (decision 7, human-approved 2026-10-02, pre-production): `crates/audio/src/denormals.rs` is the one audited `unsafe` function. It sets FTZ/DAZ (x86_64) or FZ (aarch64) on the audio processing thread, because sonora 0.2.0 AEC3 slows down on a silent far end after speech (upstream issue #34; measured 5.5× here, see Task 5). Remove it once a sonora release with upstream PR #38 ships (version bump asked first, rule 3).
- sonora 0.2.0 allocates about 40 times per 10 ms frame in AEC3 (upstream #39/#40). The processing thread is not a device callback, so this is tolerable, but not real-time clean until upstream ships the fix.

For the trace: `TWICECAT_ONNX_PROFILE=/tmp/webgpu` (ONNX Runtime's per-node JSON), `perf record --call-graph dwarf` on the `dfn_profile` binary, or Radeon GPU Profiler. Also once with `TWICECAT_WEBGPU_EP_OPTIONS="maxNumPendingDispatches=512"`. Then by ear in a call with DeepFilterNet on the GPU.
- [x] Approve the Task 13b part 4 additions: fetching Microsoft's `onnxruntime-ep-webgpu` 0.4.0 wheel from PyPI (MIT; third-party notices copied beside the library) and `GpuBackend::WebGpu` (API addition, Task 3 contract). Approved 2026-10-04.
- [x] Post the WebGPU grouped-convolution report to microsoft/onnxruntime (decided 2026-10-04; the agent drafted the text). Posted by the human 2026-10-04: https://github.com/microsoft/onnxruntime/issues/33086. No existing issue or pull request covers it (related, different: #32928, #29651, #32478). Cause, from main's source: for a channels-last grouped Conv, `conv.cc` sizes the vector width from the total output channels, while `grouped_conv.cc` derives the group once per vector. A vector spanning two groups reads the first group's inputs whenever `output_channels_per_group % components != 0` (`df_convp`: 10 outputs, 2 groups, 2 components). `preferredLayout=NCHW` is correct. JSEP's `conv-grouped.ts` has the same logic (not tested).

Also on the Frame (`scripts/profile-call-path.sh` pins and prints the Opus rows).

This is a CI runner, not the Frame itself.
- [ ] Decide the Frame's call defaults from the Opus numbers (Task 15 part 4): 64 kb/s costs less CPU than 32 kb/s on speech (20.6 against 69 µs per 10 ms here); complexity 5 halves the cost at 32 kb/s; silence (muted, gate closed) costs the most in a call (denormals on WebRTC's encoder thread). Silence options measured (Step 15: the silence options notes): not calling `capture_frame` does not stop the encoder (the source feeds zeros every 10 ms); a dither defeats DTX at complexity 9 (all packets sent, 8.5x the cost on a Zen 4 P-core) but not at c5. Still to decide with the E-core, aarch64 and Frame numbers.

- [ ] Device following on Windows by hand: `cargo run -p twicecat-audio --example device_probe -- --mic`, then change the default communications device in the Sound settings, unplug and replug a USB or Bluetooth headset, and pick a device in the app and unplug it: each time the probe prints `switched ...`, the tone carries on on the new device, the mic level follows, and the device list is printed again at once. Then the same in a call (`twicecat-cli`). Also: Windows lowers other apps' sound during a call on the communications device (Sound settings, Communications tab).
- [ ] Windows call streams (our WASAPI client) by ear: `twicecat-cli` in a call or `/mictest loopback` on headphones and on a laptop's built-in microphone and speakers. Other applications get quieter during the call when Windows' Communications setting says so; a Bluetooth headset switches to its headset profile; a driver with its own communications processing (laptop microphones) does not fight our echo cancellation and noise suppression (compare with `TWICECAT_AUDIO_BACKEND=cpal`).
- [ ] GPU enhancement on Windows by ear (the agent ran the suites on the RTX 5090): `scripts/fetch-onnxruntime.sh cuda13`, `scripts/fetch-cuda-runtime.sh`, `scripts/fetch-tensorrt.sh`, `scripts/fetch-webgpu-ep.sh`, `scripts/fetch-models.sh`, `scripts/export-deepfilternet.sh` (with uv's directories outside AppData when run from the Claude app), then the CLI built with `--features gpu-enhancement`: `/gpu`, `/ns deepfilter gpu`, `/clean gpu on`; NVIDIA's licences for fetching their Windows runtime by script, as for Linux.
- [ ] DirectML on Windows (Task 13b): on an AMD or Intel GPU, `scripts/build-onnxruntime-directml.sh`, `scripts/fetch-webgpu-ep.sh`, `scripts/fetch-models.sh`, `scripts/export-deepfilternet.sh`, then `cargo test -p twicecat-audio --release --features onnxruntime --test onnxruntime -- --ignored --nocapture` (DirectML and WebGPU both measured; `dfn_profile directml` and `dfn_profile webgpu` for the CPU per frame) and `/ns deepfilter gpu` by ear. If WebGPU is faster there at the same quality, the order (DirectML first, measured on NVIDIA) is revisited.
- [ ] Decide on a `.gitattributes` with `* text=auto eol=lf` for Windows clones (see "Windows, native").
- [ ] CLI call against a real account (`twicecat-cli`, then `/rooms`, `/call <n>`, `/who`, `/mute`, `/leave`; Element users hear you and you hear them); leave by crash (kill -9) clears the membership within the delayed-event timeout; `/mictest loopback` sounds right on headphones.

## Phase 6 call UI steps (plan docs/plans/phase6-call-ui.md, ga-lep/twicecat#14; branch feat/call-ui)
Plan approved by the human 2026-10-12 (Q1-Q8 answered: the core plays the ringtone and rings by itself; a call bar plus a participants panel; a separate Voice & audio card; a call button in every joined room; the input mode and push-to-talk are left to #15; Ctrl+Shift+M/D; the app's twicecat-audio ban is enforced by script).
- [x] 17. Call model and core events in the app (phase6.17)
- [x] 18. Room list call counts, voice rooms, the header's call button (phase6.18)
- [x] 19. In-call view: call bar, participants, mute, deafen, per-user volume and cleaning (phase6.19)
- [x] 20. Ringtone in the core (phase6.20)
- [x] 21. Incoming calls, with a stack round trip (phase6.21)
- [x] 22. Voice & audio settings (phase6.22)
- [x] 23. Wrap-up (phase6.23)

### Step 17: the call model (phase6.17)
- **Built:** `crates/app/src/calls.rs`, `CallModel` on `App` (`App::calls`):
  - the current call (`CurrentCall`: room, `CallPhase` Joining/Connected/Reconnecting, participants, people who declined);
  - call counts per room;
  - rooms that ring (one entry per room, oldest first, at most 16);
  - a call notice;
  - the last echoed `AudioSettings`, `AudioDevices`, `InputLevel`, `AudioStats` and `GpuEnhancement`.

  In `state.rs`, the arm that ignored the call and audio events now hands them to `App::on_call_event`. Login sends `GetAudioSettings` and resets the model, and logout resets it. `Error(Call | Audio | NotInCall)` on the main and loading screens becomes the call notice. On other screens it goes through the old path, so a failed restore still reaches the login screen. `CallModel` and `CurrentCall` have hand-written `Debug` impls that print counts only, no room, user, display name or reason.
- **Core API used** (rule 2, read in this tree):
  - `CallState`, `CallEndReason`, `Participant` (`crates/core/src/api/call.rs:23-86`);
  - the call and audio events (`api/mod.rs:1021-1074`);
  - `CoreError::{Call, Audio, NotInCall}` (`api/types.rs:1177-1185`);
  - `ListDiff::apply`, which ignores indexes out of range (`api/diff.rs`);
  - `AudioDevices`, `GpuEnhancement`, `InputLevel`, `AudioStats` (`api/audio.rs:155-175, 255-263, 505-545`);
  - `Command::GetAudioSettings`, answered with `AudioSettings` (`engine/mod.rs:1320`).

  Order, from the engine: no empty participant list is sent on leave, so the app clears the list on `Left`; a ring is stopped before `Connected`.
- **Behaviour choices:**
  - joining a ringing room removes its ring (the core also sends `RingingStopped`);
  - a ring for the room we are in is ignored;
  - a second ring for a room replaces the caller;
  - participants and declines for another room are ignored;
  - `Connected` or `Reconnecting` without a `Joining` starts the call;
  - a `Joining` in another room replaces the call (one call at a time, as in the core);
  - a failed call keeps "The call ended: <reason>" until the next join. Newlines show as ⏎ (`one_line_capped`), capped at 256 characters.
- **Boundary (Q2):** `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` also rejects `twicecat-audio`/`twicecat_audio`, and `scripts/check-core-api.sh` rejects `twicecat-audio` in `api/` and the public modules. Mutations (reverted): `twicecat-audio` added to the app's dependencies made the first fail; a `twicecat_audio` comment in `api/call.rs` made the second fail.
- **Tests:** 25 unit tests in `calls::tests`, driving `App::update`. They cover:
  - the state changes;
  - hang-up and failure;
  - the participant diffs, and the speaking and muted flags;
  - other rooms;
  - counts;
  - rings (cap, replacement, answered by joining);
  - declines (cap);
  - settings, devices (a hot-plug list replaces the old one), level, stats and GPU;
  - error routing;
  - logout;
  - the `Debug` output.

  `rooms_ui::settings_without_a_selected_room_do_not_open` now drains the login's `GetAudioSettings` before checking that nothing is sent. The tests are inline, not in `calls/tests.rs`, because `tests/no_secret_logging.rs` only skips `#[cfg(test)]` code inside a file. Mutations, both caught and reverted: joining no longer removing the ring (`joining_a_ringing_room_answers_it` fails); call errors not routed to the notice (`call_and_audio_errors_become_the_call_notice` fails).
- **Gate:**
  - `cargo fmt --all`: clean;
  - `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean, also with `--all-features`;
  - `cargo test --workspace`: 1593 passed, 147 ignored, 0 failed;
  - `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (also `livekit`), `scripts/check-core-api.sh`, `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh` and `scripts/check-commits.sh main`: ok.
- **Nothing on screen yet**, so no human item in this step.

### Step 18: room list and header (phase6.18)
- **Built:**
  - **Room list rows** (`screens/room_list_pane.rs`). A "Voice" tag for MSC3417 call rooms (`RoomSummary::is_call_room`). A call pill (`calls::CallPill`, "🔊 N") before the unread badge whenever the room's call has devices (`ActiveCall`) or the room rings. It is in the highlight colour while the room rings and an outline otherwise. Without a call the pill is an empty space, so a row keeps its widget types, as `badge_view` does.
  - **Room header** (`screens/main_view.rs`). Every joined room has a call button after Members and Settings (plan Q6). Spaces and invitations have none. The button comes from `CallModel::button`:
    - "Call" (primary), or "Join call (N)" while a call has devices;
    - "Join voice" or "Join voice (N)" in a voice room;
    - "Joining…" (off) while joining;
    - "Leave call" (danger) while in this room's call, also while reconnecting.
  - **`Message::Call(CallMessage::{Join(room), Leave})`** sends `JoinCall { room_id, intent: Audio }` or `LeaveCall`. `CallModel::pending` keeps a second press, and any other join or leave, from sending until the core answers. Any `CallState` or a call error (`Call`, `Audio`, `NotInCall`) clears it. A join for an invitation or the current call's room sends nothing; so does a leave without a call. A room missing from `App::rooms` is still joined, because that list is filtered by the search and the tabs (the core answers an unknown room with `Joining` and then `Left { Failed }`). A voice room never joins on its own; joining is always a click.
- **iced API** (rule 2, `iced_widget` 0.14.2 as locked): `button::primary` (`src/button.rs:598`), `button::danger` (:658), `Button::on_press_maybe` (:170). `button::success` (:628) was not used: no app colour test covers it, and the theme's contrast rule is checked for primary and danger only.
- **Tests:** 10 more in `calls::tests`, plus one row build test in `room_list_pane`. They cover:
  - the labels and presses through every call state;
  - voice rooms;
  - no button for spaces or invitations;
  - one `JoinCall` per press until answered, with the other rooms' buttons off meanwhile;
  - a refused join tried again;
  - the joins that send nothing;
  - one `LeaveCall`, and the button after `Left`;
  - the pill's count and ring;
  - `App::view` building in every call state.

  Mutation, caught and reverted: without the pending check, `a_join_sends_one_command_until_the_core_answers` and `a_leave_sends_one_command_until_the_call_is_left` fail.
- **Gate:** fmt, clippy (also `--all-features`), `cargo test --workspace` 1603 passed / 0 failed / 147 ignored, and the dependency, API, no-download and commit checks: ok.
- **Human items:** the pill, the Voice tag and the header button in light and dark themes (Phase 6 call UI checkpoint).

### Step 19: the in-call view (phase6.19)
- **Built:**
  - **Call bar** (`screens/call.rs::bar`, the last child of the room list pane's column, an empty space without a call or a notice). It shows:
    - the call's room name, as a button that opens the room with the panel (`ShowCall`);
    - a lock when the media is encrypted;
    - the phase: "Connecting…", "Voice connected[, encrypted]", or "Reconnecting (attempt n)…" in the danger colour;
    - who declined our ring (three names, then "and N more");
    - **Mute**/**Unmute**, **Deafen**/**Undeafen** (primary while on), and **Leave** (danger, off while a join or leave is in flight);
    - the call notice with a ✕ (`DismissNotice`).
  - **Participants panel** (`screens/call.rs::panel_slot`, a fifth slot of the panes row after the thread panel, 320 px). It shows only while it is open and the call's room is the open room. It opens:
    - from the header's **People** button, shown in the call's room only (`TogglePanel`);
    - from the call bar;
    - on its own when joining a voice room (plan Q4).

    It closes with ✕, Esc (after the thread panel), or the call's end. Rows come from `CallModel::rows`:
    - the picture from the call room's member list (the initials fallback while it is unknown), ringed in the highlight colour while speaking;
    - the name: the call's display name, else the member list's, else the user ID; one line, capped;
    - "(you)", 🔇, and "Speaking" / "Muted" as words (not colour alone);
    - the cleaning status ("Voice cleaned", "Not cleaned (too many voices)", …);
    - for other people, on their first device's row only (the core's settings are per user): **−** / **+** (10 %), a 0–200 % slider (step 5, Shift 1, double-click back to 100), the value, and a "Clean: automatic/always/never" menu.
  - **Mute and deafen** (`ToggleMute`, `ToggleDeafen`; Ctrl+Shift+M and Ctrl+Shift+D, only during a call, and taken by the composer's key binding when it has focus) send `SetMicMuted` and `SetDeafened`. The core keeps the two flags apart (`engine/mod.rs:1369-1384`, `apply_mute` at :1849: the effective mute is mic_muted || deafened). So undeafening restores the earlier mute by itself, and unmuting while deafened sends `SetDeafened(false)` then `SetMicMuted(false)`, as Discord does. Our own row shows muted at once, from the local flags. Both reset on `Joining`, as in the core.
  - **Volume.** A slider move only updates `volume_drag` and starts a 400 ms timer. The value is sent once with `SetUserVolume`, on release or when the latest timer fires (stale sequence numbers do nothing). Wheel and arrow-key moves never report a release, hence the timer. A value equal to the core's is not sent; **−**/**+** send at once, clamped to 0..=200. Nothing is sent for ourselves or for someone not in the call.
  - **Cleaning.** `SetUserCleaning` is sent when the choice differs from the echoed settings' `user_cleaning`.
  - **Members.** `Joining` sends `ListRoomMembers` for the call's room. `App::on_room_members` hands the answer to the call model whatever room is open. Then:
    - the participants' pictures are fetched (`FetchAvatars`, each once per session);
    - a newcomer missing from the list asks for it again, once per user;
    - nothing more is asked while the first answer is on its way.
- **iced API** (rule 2, `iced_widget` 0.14.2):
  - `slider(range, value, on_change)` with `T: Copy + From<u8> + PartialOrd` (`src/helpers.rs:1534`, `src/slider.rs:100-117`), plus `.step`, `.shift_step`, `.default` and `.on_release` (:151-188). Arrow keys work only with the cursor over it (:393-405), and the wheel changes it without a release (:375-390).
  - `pick_list` (`src/helpers.rs:1656`), `rule::vertical`, `scrollable`.
- **Tests:** 25 more in `calls::tests` and 2 in `screens::call::tests`. They cover:
  - mute and deafen commands, unmute while deafened, undeafen keeping the mute, the reset on a new call, nothing without a call;
  - the shortcuts (in a call only, in the table, taken by the composer);
  - the members request on join; names and pictures from a member list of a room that is not open (the open room's list untouched); a newcomer asking once; no second request before the first answer;
  - the rows (our mute, speaking, cleaning words, one set of controls per user, deafened shows muted);
  - the volume (release sends once, the latest pause sends, a stale pause does nothing, an unchanged value sends nothing, the steps clamp, nobody else or ourselves);
  - the cleaning menu;
  - the panel (voice room opens it, ordinary does not, beside the call's room only, `ShowCall` opens the room, closes on `Left`, Esc);
  - the notice ✕;
  - labels.

  In `state.rs`:
  - the panes-row test now expects five slots and checks that the participants panel leaves the room pane's tree alone;
  - a new layout test shows the panel taking exactly its 320 px from the room pane, the room list keeping its width with the call bar and a long notice, and the window still filled.

  Mutation, caught and reverted: controls on every device's row (`rows_mark_…_once_per_person` fails).
- **Docs:** `docs/accessibility.md` covers the two shortcuts, the Esc order, the call controls, the slider's keyboard limit, and the "not colour alone" marks.
- **Gate:** fmt, clippy (also `--all-features`), `cargo test --workspace` 1630 passed / 0 failed / 147 ignored, and the dependency, API, no-download and commit checks: ok.

### Step 20: the ringtone in the core (phase6.20)
- **API addition** (approved in plan Q3b):
  - `Command::SilenceRinging`;
  - `AudioSettings::ringtone: bool`, default `true`. Older `call_settings.json` files read as `true` through the struct's `#[serde(default)]`, with no version bump.
  - The rustdoc of `CoreEvent::IncomingCall` says when the tone plays.
  - `scripts/check-core-api.sh` passes.
- **Built:**
  - **The watcher.** `call/watcher.rs`: `Ring` gains `loud` (from the notification's `ring`) and `silenced`. `WatchState` gains a `watch::Sender<bool>`, "a loud, unsilenced ring exists". It is republished by `sync_loud` after every change to the rings: ring, take, decline on another device, the call ending or joined on another device, expiry, silence. All removals go through `remove_ring`. `CallWatch::loud()` subscribes, `CallWatch::silence()` silences the rings sounding now (later rings sound again), and `CallWatch::stop` sends `false` first.
  - **The tone.** New `crates/core/src/ringtone.rs`. `Tone` is a `twicecat_audio::FrameProcessor`: 440 + 480 Hz, 1 s on and 2 s off, 10 ms fades, each sine at 0.15 × master volume / 100 (peak 0.3, about −10 dBFS at 100 %).
  - **The ringer.** `Ringer::start(loud, gate, backend)` runs a task that opens a playback-only `AudioIo` (`IoConfig { capture: false, playback: true, output_device, .. }`, or `start_virtual` for `AudioBackend::Virtual`) while `loud` and the gate's `playable()` agree. It reopens the device when the gate's device or volume changes, and closes it otherwise. Opening and closing run in `spawn_blocking`, because dropping an `AudioIo` joins its thread. A failure to open is logged (fixed text) and retried on the next change. `Ringer::stop` closes the device and ends the task, and so does the watch closing.
  - **The engine.** It keeps `ring_gate: watch::Sender<RingGate>` (in a call, `ringtone`, output device, master volume) and updates it on:
    - joining (in a call from `Joining` on);
    - a failed join, a leave, a lost call server;
    - the settings' first load and every `store_audio_settings`.

    Each session's `Syncing` owns a `Ringer`, started with the `CallWatch` and stopped right after it in `stop_tasks` (logout, shutdown). `SilenceRinging` calls `CallWatch::silence`; with nothing ringing it does nothing.
  - **CLI.** `/silence` and `/ringtone [on|off]`, with help lines. `/audio` adds "Ringtone on/off." The incoming-call line adds "/silence to stop the ringtone".
- **APIs read** (rule 2):
  - `twicecat_audio::FrameProcessor` (`crates/audio/src/pipeline.rs:22`).
  - `AudioIo::start`, which refuses only when nothing is open (`io/mod.rs:148-150`); with no capture, a 10 ms timer paces processing (`pipeline.rs:8`). `AudioIo::start_virtual` (:237), and the `Drop` that joins the thread (:316).
  - tokio 1.53.1 `watch::Sender::{send_if_modified (sync/watch.rs:1171), send_replace (:1229), subscribe (:1387)}`, `Default` (:211), `Receiver::{borrow_and_update (:676), changed (:818)}`.
- **Tests:**
  - **Unit, `ringtone::tests` (8).** The cycle (on, silent rest, the next cycle), the fades (first sample 0, largest step < 0.03), the volume (200 % twice 100 %, 0 % silent), the gate. Ringer runs against a virtual speaker: a loud ring plays until it stops; no tone in a call or when turned off, and joining a call stops it; a new volume reopens it and `stop` closes it; the watch closing ends it. These are timing-based on the virtual 10 ms clock, so they poll with 5 s deadlines; 20 runs in a row passed.
  - **Unit, `call::watcher::tests` (3).** Only loud rings sound; silencing keeps the ring and a later ring sounds; only a change wakes the receiver.
  - **Unit, `call_settings` (2).** A file without `ringtone` reads as on; `ringtone: false` round-trips.
  - **The serde shape lock** `api::audio::tests::serde_shape_is_stable` now includes `"ringtone": true`. It caught the format change in the gate, as it should.
  - **CLI.** Parsing of `/ringtone` and `/silence`; `/ringtone` toggles and sets.
  - **Stack** (`crates/core/tests/call_ringing.rs`, ignored, carol on a counting virtual speaker):
    - `a_dm_ring_plays_the_ringtone_until_silenced`: the tone reaches the speaker; `SilenceRinging` closes it; the ring can still be declined, and alice gets `CallDeclined`.
    - `answering_stops_the_ringtone`: nothing audible for 3.2 s, longer than the tone's 2 s rest, once connected.

    Mutation, caught and reverted: `SilenceRinging` doing nothing makes the first test time out.
- **Local stack runs:**
  - `scripts/stack.sh up -d --wait`, `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, then `cargo test -p twicecat-core --test call_ringing -- --ignored --test-threads=1`: 6 passed.
  - The full ignored run, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`: 145 passed, 7 failed. The 7 are all marked "needs scripts/fetch-models.sh", and the DeepFilterNet models are not fetched on this machine: `twicecat-audio` `deepfilter` (3), `call::cleaning::tests::the_cpu_filters_take_over_after_a_give_up`, and `call_media`'s `remote_voices_are_cleaned_when_asked`, `voices_cleaned_at_their_source_are_left_alone`, `without_a_gpu_voices_are_cleaned_on_the_cpu`. The models were not fetched, because that is a download.
  - Every other call suite (`call_media`, `call_signalling`, `call_settings`, `cli`) passed with the ringer running in each session, so the tone does not reach the media tests' virtual devices.
- **Gate** (with this step's changes only): fmt, clippy (also `--all-features`), `cargo test --workspace` 1643 passed / 0 failed / 149 ignored, and the dependency, API (with the new API), no-download and commit checks: ok.

### Step 21: incoming calls (phase6.21)
- **Built:**
  - **Incoming-call card** (`screens/call.rs::ring_layer`). It is the outermost layer of the main screen, above verification, always a stack of two, a dimmed modal (`room_dialogs::modal`). It shows the oldest DM ring (`ring: true`) whose card was not hidden (`CallModel::card`):
    - the caller's picture (the DM's avatar) and name (the DM's name, else the caller's ID), with "in <room>" for a group room;
    - **Decline** (danger) and **Accept** (primary);
    - "Esc hides this; the room keeps ringing."
  - **Accept** sends `JoinCall` through the same path as the header. **Decline** sends `DeclineCall`. Each sends once: `Ring::answered` turns the buttons off until the core answers, and a call error turns them back on (a refused decline: "could not send the decline"). The card hides while that room's call is joining or running. The ring itself stays until `RingingStopped`, which the core sends when the join succeeds. A failed join (`Left`) shows the card again with its buttons on, as the core's ringtone sounds again (review fix, step 23).
  - **Esc** (first in `on_escape`, before the switcher and every dialog) hides the card of every DM ring (`Ring::hidden`; the core's `SilenceRinging` silences them all) and sends `SilenceRinging`. The rooms keep their highlighted pills and the header's join button. A later ring sounds and shows its card (review fix, step 23). A new `IncomingCall` for the same room (a new call) shows its card again.
  - **While the card shows**, `any_overlay_open` and `settings::other_modal_open` count it, so the room shortcuts wait.
  - **A new ring** (not a replay, not for the call we are in) asks for window attention: `Critical` for a DM ring (until focused), `Informational` for a group call (`state::attention_at`). It also shows a desktop notification through `App::notify`, which follows the same `should_show` rule as messages (setting on, window not focused). The text comes from `notifications::call_toast`:
    - the title is the room's name;
    - with "Show message content" on, the body is "<caller> is calling you" or "<caller> started a call";
    - with it off, the body is "Incoming call" or "A call started", without the caller;
    - everything is sanitised and markup-escaped like message notifications.

    A click opens the room (the existing `OpenRoomFromNotification`).
- **iced API** (rule 2): `window::UserAttention::{Critical, Informational}` (`iced_core` 0.14.0 `src/window/user_attention.rs:10-20`: Critical keeps flashing until focus on Windows and bounces until focus on macOS).
- **Tests:** 11 more in `calls::tests`, plus `call_toasts_name_the_caller_only_with_content_on` and `the_ring_card_names_the_caller_and_a_group_room`. They cover:
  - a DM ring opens the card and notifies once, and a replay adds nothing;
  - a group call notifies quietly with no card;
  - a focused window or notifications off show no notification, but the card still shows;
  - with content off, the caller stays out of the notification;
  - a ring for our own call announces nothing;
  - Accept joins once and the card goes on `Joining`;
  - Decline sends once and the card goes on `RingingStopped`;
  - a refused answer can be tried again;
  - a ring in a room hidden by the search can be accepted;
  - Esc hides the card and silences, the next card shows, and a new call shows the card again;
  - the card closes before Settings.
- **Stack round trip with `twicecat-cli`** (new `crates/app/tests/calls.rs`, ignored). Alice is the real `twicecat-cli` binary (`--ephemeral --silent-audio`, found next to the test binary in `target/<profile>/`), driven through stdin. Carol is the app on the real `Bridge` with silent virtual audio, logged in through the login form. The DM is made over plain HTTP against the local homeserver only, with alice's `m.direct` naming it so that her call rings. The test:
  - alice `/call`, and the app shows the card with `ring: true` from alice;
  - **Decline**, and the CLI prints "@carol:… declined the call in"; the ring stops in the app;
  - alice leaves and calls again; **Accept**; the app is `Connected` with itself and alice (alice's row has controls); the CLI prints "… joined the call.";
  - the app leaves, and the CLI prints "… left the call.".
- **Runs:**
  - `cargo test -p twicecat-app --test calls -- --ignored`: passed 4 times in a row, about 8 s each.
  - Mutation, caught and reverted: **Decline** sending nothing makes the CLI never print the decline.
  - Gate: fmt, clippy (also `--all-features`), `cargo test --workspace` 1656 passed / 0 failed / 150 ignored, and the dependency, API, no-download and commit checks: ok.
- **Docs:** `docs/accessibility.md` covers the card in the Esc order, its buttons, and that a ring is never only a sound.

### Step 22: the Voice & audio card (phase6.22)
- **Built.** New `crates/app/src/audio_settings.rs` (model, `AudioMessage`, `App::on_audio`) and `screens/audio_settings.rs`. The card is a modal layer above Settings and below the media viewer. It opens from Settings ("Voice & audio…", which closes Settings, as "Manage sessions…" does; plan Q5) and from the call bar's **Audio…**. Opening sends `GetAudioSettings`, `ListAudioDevices` (which also starts the core's hot-plug watcher) and `ProbeGpuEnhancement`. Esc or **Close** closes it, after sending a moved slider and stopping the mic test it started. The card shows the last echoed `AudioSettings`. Every change copies it, changes one field, and sends the whole value with `UpdateAudioSettings`. An unchanged value sends nothing. Before the first echo a change asks for the settings again. The input mode and push-to-talk bindings pass through untouched (plan Q7, #15).
  - **Devices.** Microphone and speakers menus: "System default" (`None`) and the listed devices. A saved device that is not listed stays chosen, as "<name> (not connected)", using names seen this session, else "Saved device". During a call: "A new device is used from the next call." (the core's behaviour).
  - **Microphone.** Noise suppression: Off / WebRTC / RNNoise / DeepFilterNet3. Picking the one already set keeps its options; WebRTC starts at Moderate, DeepFilterNet3 at 100 dB without the GPU. Then:
    - WebRTC's strength menu, or DeepFilterNet3's limit slider;
    - "Use the GPU" only when `GpuEnhancement::available` is not empty, where it runs (`GpuEnhancement::running`), and the RNNoise fallback note;
    - echo cancellation, and "Also remove other apps' sound", which is disabled without echo cancellation; turning echo cancellation off turns it off too;
    - automatic gain and high-pass;
    - the note on what runs together and what excludes, as the core's docs ask.
  - **Levels.** Microphone gain (−20…20 dB, step 0.5), volume (0…200 %, step 5), and voice quality (16–128 kb/s; an unusual current value up to 510 stays in the menu).
  - **Mic test.** Start/Stop with "Hear myself (use headphones)"; changing it during a test restarts the test. Off during a call, with the reason; a `Joining` ends the card's test (the call takes the devices). A level bar from `InputLevel` (−60…0 dBFS) with "N dB, peak N dB, sending/not sending".
  - **Ringtone.** "Play a ringtone when someone calls you directly" (`AudioSettings::ringtone`, step 20).

  - **Your microphone's cleaning.** "Tell others my microphone is cleaned", with the privacy note (everyone in the call and the call server's operator can see which suppressor you use), and "cleaned outside Twicecat" (off unless announcing).
  - **Diagnostics** (hidden by default), from `AudioStats`: echo removed, echo left, echo delay, processing µs, late wake-ups, over- and underruns.
  - **Sliders** send on release or after a 400 ms pause, as in the participants panel. **−**/**+** beside each send one step at once, clamped. The echo always wins, so a clamped value snaps back.
  - **Settings.** The push-to-talk placeholder becomes a "Voice & audio" section, and `PUSH_TO_TALK_LINE` now reads "Push-to-talk arrives in a later version." (#15).
- **iced API** (rule 2, `iced_widget` 0.14.2): `checkbox(is_checked).label().on_toggle()` and `on_toggle_maybe` (`src/checkbox.rs:119-167`); `progress_bar(range, value)` (`src/helpers.rs:1779`) with `.girth` (`src/progress_bar.rs:97`).

### Fix: an expired session ends the call and the mic test (found by the branch review)
- On `SessionChange::UnknownToken`, the engine used to stop the session's tasks without leaving the call or stopping the audio session. This was already the case on `main`.
- As a result, a revoked token during a call left the call's media and the microphone running behind the login screen. Since this branch, the ringtone's gate also stayed "in a call" until the next call.
- Now `Engine::end_session_audio` (leave the call, stop the mic test) runs first. If the dead token cannot remove the membership, the server's delayed leave removes it.
- Test: `the_end_of_a_session_stops_the_mic_test`, an engine with virtual audio.

### Step 23: wrap-up (phase6.23)
- **Branch review** by an independent read-only review agent over `main..HEAD`. It verified 10 defects; privacy was clean (the hand-written `Debug` impls print counts only, the tracing lines are fixed text, call notifications hide the caller without "Show message content"). The fixes were folded into the commits that wrote the code (rule 5: `--fixup`, `git rebase --autosquash main`, a clean rebase, the final tree identical to the unsquashed one, and each rewritten commit's lib tests run again):
  1. *An expired session left the call and the mic test running* (already on `main`): its own `fix(core)` commit, see above.
  2. *A failed Accept left the ringtone sounding with nothing on screen* (the app dropped the ring on `Joining`, the core only takes it on success): fixed in step 21. The ring stays until `RingingStopped`, its card hides while that room's call is joining or running, and `Left` turns its buttons back on. Test: `a_failed_accept_shows_the_card_again`.
  3. *Quick edits overwrote each other* (each built on the last echo, and no echo comes while a join holds the engine): fixed in steps 19 and 22. Every edit (card, volume, cleaning) is kept in the local settings at once and the echo replaces it, so two **+** presses give 110 then 120. Tests: `two_changes_before_the_echo_both_count`, `steps_before_the_echo_add_up`, `a_cleaning_choice_is_kept_before_its_echo`.
  4. *Answering a ring while in another call opened the gate for a moment* (a burst of tone): fixed in step 20. `join_call` closes the gate before leaving the old call (`end_call(true)`).
  5. *A refused mic test still looked like it was running*: fixed in step 22. An `Audio` error clears it, and the card shows the call notice (which the card covers in the call bar). Test: `a_refused_mic_test_is_not_shown_as_running`.
  6. *Esc closed the audio card under the switcher, the upload dialog or the viewer*: fixed in step 22. Esc now follows the layer order. Test: `escape_follows_the_layers_over_the_card`.
  7. *Esc silenced every ring but hid one card*: fixed in step 21. Every DM ring's card hides with the silence.
  8. *`pending` and `answered` are cleared by any call state or call error, and `NotLoggedIn` is not an answer*: **open, low.** No path leaves them stuck on the main screen; logout resets them.
  9. *A second person's slider dropped the first one's value*: fixed in step 19. The first is sent when another starts moving. Test: `a_second_persons_slider_sends_the_first_ones_value`.
  10. *An early ring could ignore the stored ringtone setting*: fixed in step 20. `start_sync` loads the settings before the ringer starts. Test: `the_stored_ringtone_setting_reaches_the_gate_when_loaded`.

  The review also asked for member names in the declined line, done in step 19 ("Bob declined", the user ID until known).
- **Issue #14, item by item:**
  - **Room header** join/leave and the room list call badge, voice rooms included: step 18.
  - **In-call view:**
    - participants with name, picture, speaking and muted, plus mute and deafen (`SetDeafened`), per-user volume (0–200 %) and per-user cleaning: step 19;
    - call state and errors: steps 17 and 19.

    All in step 22. The input mode and push-to-talk are left to #15 (plan Q7).

### Fix: a mic test asked for during a call no longer cuts the call (found at the wrap-up, done at the human's request)
- `Engine::start_mic_test` stopped the running audio session before it checked for a call. This was already the case on `main`. During a call that session is the call's audio, so a `StartMicTest` mid-call (the CLI's `/mictest`; the app's button is off in a call) silenced the call's speakers and microphone, then answered "not available during a call".
- Now the refusal comes first. It also counts an audio session in `Mode::Call` as a call, which covers the time between `start_call_audio` and the call being set.
- Tests:
  - `engine::tests::a_mic_test_during_a_call_keeps_the_calls_audio`: a virtual call-mode session stays, and the error is sent.
  - The ignored stack test `call_media::a_mic_test_during_a_call_leaves_the_call_playing`: carol hears alice's 1 kHz tone, asks for a mic test, is refused, and still hears the tone in the samples played after the refusal.
- Mutation, caught by both and reverted: stopping the session before the check. The stack test then fails with "the speakers stopped: 0 new samples in 2 s", which is the original bug.
- Checks: fmt, both clippy runs, `cargo test --workspace` (1690 passed, 151 ignored), the boundary, API, WebRTC and commit scripts all ok. The full ignored run on the local stack passed 147 tests. The only failures were the 7 tests that need `scripts/fetch-models.sh` (the DeepFilterNet models), which was not run here.

### Light and full builds, with RustRover run configurations (2026-10-12, at the human's request)
- **Why:** the Voice & audio card offered no GPU. The GPU path is behind the core's `gpu-enhancement` feature, and the app had no way to turn it on, so the probe always answered "none" and the card hid the option without a word.
- **The `gpu` feature:** `twicecat-app` has a `gpu` feature (`twicecat-core/gpu-enhancement`). The light build is the default (CPU only); `--features gpu` is the full build.
- **The card note:** with DeepFilterNet3 chosen and no GPU offered, the card now says why (`no_gpu_note`): the light build has no GPU support, or the full build found no usable GPU (pointing to `docs/building.md`). Before the probe answers it says nothing. Test: `the_card_says_why_no_gpu_is_offered`, run in both builds.
- **RustRover:** two shared run configurations in `.run/`, **Twicecat full (GPU)** and **Twicecat light (CPU only)**, both release builds. The format is the one RustRover 2026.2.4 writes itself: the human ran one, and RustRover rewrote it with `buildProfileId` `release`, a `file://$PROJECT_DIR$` working directory, `buildTarget` `REMOTE` and no final newline. Both files now match that form, so the IDE leaves them alone. The type, factory, option names and enum values had been checked against its plugin classes before.
- **Docs:** `docs/building.md` has both builds and the GPU parts the full build loads at run time.
- **Checks:** both commands build; the gate was clean (1691 tests).

### The card says what runs on the GPU, and on which backend (2026-10-12, at the human's request)
- **Why:** in the full build, the GPU still sat behind a checkbox that appeared only once DeepFilterNet3 was chosen, so the human did not find it. The human also asked to tell NVIDIA's backends from the generic ones.
- **The menu:**
  - Every noise suppression entry says where it runs: "WebRTC (light, CPU)", "RNNoise (CPU, default)", "DeepFilterNet3 (best, CPU)".
  - With a GPU found, there is also **DeepFilterNet3 on the GPU (…)**, naming the backend it starts on. The probe lists backends in the core's order of preference, so the first one is used.
  - The entry replaces the old "Use the GPU" checkbox (`MicCleaningGpu` is gone). Moving between the CPU and GPU entries keeps the limit.
  - A GPU setting from another machine keeps its entry.
- **Backend names:** by vendor and API. "NVIDIA · TensorRT" and "NVIDIA · CUDA"; "any GPU · WebGPU on Vulkan" (on Direct3D 12 on Windows, on Metal on macOS); "any GPU · DirectML"; "Apple · Core ML". They appear in the menu, in "Clean on the GPU (…)" and in the "Running on the GPU (…)" notes.
- **The GPU line**, under the menu, always shown: "looking for one…", "X is used. Also here: Y.", or why there is none (light build, or nothing usable found).
- **Tests:** `the_menu_names_the_gpu_backend_that_runs`, `moving_deepfilter_between_cpu_and_gpu_keeps_its_limit`, `the_card_says_why_no_gpu_is_offered`, and the settings cases with the GPU entry. They pass in both builds; the gate was clean (1694 tests).

### Automatic gain's most boost (2026-10-12, human request)

- **Asked:** a slider for each processing option where it makes sense. Only automatic gain has a setting worth one:
  - AEC3 has no strength (it measures the echo path itself); its only option, the transparent-mode algorithm, is internal.
  - sonora's high-pass filter has a fixed cutoff; a cutoff slider would need our own filter. Left as a switch.
- **Core:** `AudioProcessing::auto_gain_max_db` (1..=50 dB, `limits::AUTO_GAIN_MAX_DB`; AGC2 refuses 0), default 30 dB instead of AGC2's 50, so a quiet room is not lifted as far. Older settings files read as 30. The chain passes it to AGC2's adaptive digital controller as `max_gain_db`, with its start gain (15 dB) capped to it, and reconfigures AGC2 only when it changes while on.
- **App:** under **Automatic gain**, a **Most boost** slider while it is on; while it is off, a note points to **Microphone gain** under Levels.
- **CLI:** `/agc <dB>` sets it and turns automatic gain on; `/audio` shows it.
- **Fix in the card:** "Without its model, RNNoise runs instead" showed while DeepFilterNet3 ran. It now shows only until the card knows where DeepFilterNet3 runs; a missing model is still reported as an error when a call or mic test starts.
- **Tests:** `auto_gain_boosts_no_more_than_its_most` (a 6 dB most holds a quiet signal at 6 dB; 40 dB lifts it further), `auto_gain_settings_reach_agc2`, `the_most_boost_follows_its_slider_and_steps`, the validation and serde cases, and the CLI's `/agc` parse and settings cases. The gate was clean (1698 tests).

### Step 24: toggle-to-talk in the core (phase6.24)
- **Core:** `InputMode::PushToTalk { release_delay_ms, toggle }`. `toggle` is `#[serde(default)]`, so files written before it read as push-to-talk. The gate ignores it: the app turns presses into on and off and still sends `Command::PushToTalk` (plan Q2). `validated` keeps it.
- **CLI:** `/gate toggle [ms]` beside `/gate ptt [ms]`; without a number both keep the current release delay (200 ms coming from voice activity). `/audio` says "toggle-to-talk, N ms release". `/ptt` is unchanged: a terminal sees no releases, so it already toggles.
- **Tests:** `toggle_to_talk_round_trips` (serde shape), `settings_without_bindings_still_load` (an old file is push-to-talk), `validated_clamps_every_field` and the stack-free `update_is_validated_saved_and_survives_a_restart` keep `toggle`, `settings_map_onto_the_chain` (the gate is the same with `toggle`), and the CLI's parse, settings and display cases.

### Step 25: the push-to-talk service in the app (phase6.25)
- **Dependency (Q4):** `crates/app` depends on `twicecat-ptt` (path). `Cargo.lock` only gains that edge. `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` now also checks `twicecat-ptt`'s whole tree (normal and build edges, all features and targets) for the SDK crates, `twicecat-audio`, `twicecat-core`, `livekit`, `libwebrtc` and `webrtc-sys`. Mutation: a `twicecat-core` dependency added to `twicecat-ptt` failed the script (exit 1), then reverted.
- **`twicecat-ptt` additions** (`binding.rs`): `Held` (the window's modifiers), `KeyBinding::from_key(code, held)` (a W3C code name such as `KeyT` plus modifiers; a modifier key alone or a key global-hotkey cannot name is refused), `is_key(code)` and `has_modifiers(held)`. `PttConfig` derives `PartialEq, Eq`.
- **`crates/app/src/ptt.rs` (new), `PttModel` on `App`:**
  - `setup_for(settings, keyboard)`: the bindings mapped to `PttConfig`, or `None` for voice activity. A saved binding that does not parse is left out and named (`BadBinding::Key`/`Button`), never with its text.
  - `App::sync_ptt` runs after every message, in `update`: the service runs only on the main screen, in push-to-talk or toggle mode, during a call, the mic test or with the Voice & audio card open (Q1). Same setup: nothing happens; a changed key, button or mode restarts it; otherwise it is dropped. The release delay is the core's, so changing it restarts nothing. Logout and shutdown leave the main screen, so the service is dropped in `update` before the window goes.
  - **Forwarding:** the sink only sends on a std channel. One thread (`ptt-forward`, spawned on the first start) turns `Transmit` into `Command::PushToTalk` with `blocking_send`, so a release waits for room instead of being dropped, and then tells the window (an unbounded channel read by a subscription). `update` is never in that path. `Talk` holds or toggles (a press flips; releases are ignored). Each start has a generation, and a stopped service's late events are dropped. Stopping the service, or a new audio session (call or mic test starting or ending), lets go: `PushToTalk { pressed: false }` if it was on.
  - **Window key:** a second `listen_with` is subscribed only while a service runs. Presses (not repeats) and releases of identified keys become `Message::Ptt(WindowKey)`, also when a widget captured the key (a focused composer still talks). A press needs the binding's exact modifiers; a release does not (a modifier may be let go first). `WindowFocus(false)` lets go.
  - `Ready`/`Unavailable` are kept per source, for the card (step 27). `talking` follows what was sent to the core.
  - `CommandSink` gains `command_sender()` and `runtime()`: `Bridge` gives the core's channel and runtime (`ServiceStarter` starts `PttService` on it). The test sink gives its channel and no runtime, and tests install a fake starter with `App::set_ptt_starter`.
- **APIs read** (rule 2): `iced_core-0.14.0` `keyboard/event.rs:12-55` (`KeyPressed`/`KeyReleased`), `keyboard/key.rs:849` (`Code`), `:1325` (`Physical`), `keyboard/modifiers.rs:48-69`; `iced_futures-0.14.0/src/event.rs:26` (`listen_with`); `iced-0.14.0/src/advanced.rs:4` (`into_recipes`, tests only); `futures-channel-0.3.34/src/mpsc/mod.rs:420` (`unbounded`), `:1262` (`try_recv`); `tokio-1.53.1/src/sync/mpsc/bounded.rs:1046` (`blocking_send`, on a plain thread); `global-hotkey-0.8.0/src/hotkey.rs:168-233` (parsing: one token is a key; names are case-insensitive W3C codes).
- **Tests** (12 in `ptt::tests`, 2 in `twicecat_ptt::binding`): bindings to config (voice activity, none bound, bad key, bad button); hold and toggle; when the service runs (card, call, mic test, voice activity, logout); a changed binding restarts once and the delay does not; `Transmit` reaching the command channel in order while the test never calls `update`; toggle flipping and letting go on leave; a new audio session letting go; window keys (modifiers on press only, repeats, other keys, focus loss, no key bound); events to messages; sources per source with stale ones dropped after a restart; the key subscription only while running; the forwarding thread ending with the app. The fake's drop asserts it runs on the thread that started it.
- **Known:** dropping the service joins the hotkey thread (it checks for a stop every 200 ms) and the gamepad thread (100 ms), so leaving a call or changing a binding can hold `update` up to about 200 ms. Joining keeps an old hotkey thread from taking the new one's events from global-hotkey's shared channel.

### Step 26: the release tail end to end (phase6.26)
- **`crates/app/tests/ptt.rs` (new), not ignored:** it needs no homeserver. The real `Bridge` and core run with `AudioBackend::Virtual` (silence). The app is put on its main screen with made-up session events, and only audio events are passed to it. A fake service gives the test its sink. Settings go to the core directly; the card and the mic test are opened through `App::update`, which starts the (fake) service.
  - `a_release_keeps_transmitting_for_the_delay_then_stops`: 1000 ms delay. Closed until pressed; a press reaches the core through the forwarding thread and the mic test's `InputLevel::transmitting` turns on; after the release it is still on at least 500 ms of audio later and off between 0.7 and 3 s of audio (measured in the frames the virtual microphone captured; on the clock it can take longer on a busy runner).
  - `without_a_delay_a_release_stops_at_once_and_voice_activity_ignores_presses`: 0 ms delay closes within 500 ms; in voice activity, with a silent microphone, a press opens nothing for 500 ms.
- **Runs:** 10 in a row, all passed (about 1.2 s each). Mutation: the forwarding thread always sending `pressed: true` fails both tests; reverted.

### Step 27: the Talking section of the Voice & audio card (phase6.27)
- **Talking** (under Devices). A **Send** menu: voice activity, push-to-talk (held), toggle-to-talk (press to start, press to stop). Between push-to-talk and toggle-to-talk the release delay is kept; coming from voice activity it starts at 200 ms; going back to voice activity the level is automatic. The bindings are always kept.
  - **Voice activity:** "Set the level automatically", and when off, a **Send above** slider (−100…0 dB, step 1, with **−**/**+**). A note says the mic test's level line shows when the voice would be sent.
  - **Push-to-talk and toggle-to-talk:**
    - **Key:** the binding as people write it (`Ctrl+Shift+T`, `Super`/`Cmd`), with **Set key…** and **Clear**. While waiting, the row says "Press the key, with its modifiers (Esc cancels)…" with **Cancel**, and the app's key listener is swapped for a capture-only one, so no shortcut (Esc included) is made of the keys and the two never race. A modifier alone keeps waiting; Esc, **Cancel**, closing the card or the window losing focus stop waiting; a key global-hotkey cannot name shows "This key cannot be used for push-to-talk. Try another, or Esc." The old key's window source is off meanwhile.
    - **Gamepad button:** a menu (Q3): None, then the 19 positions with what common pads print on them ("Bottom face button (A, ✕)", "Left bumper (LB, L1)"…). A saved button Twicecat does not know shows as "Saved button (not recognised)".
    - **Release delay** slider (0…2000 ms, step 50), with why it exists.
    - A note on the session's keyboard mechanism (global shortcut, Wayland portal, or window only), saved bindings that cannot be used, and one line per source from the running service ("Desktop shortcut: ready, press Ctrl+Shift+T.", "Gamepad: not available (…)."). Reasons and triggers are capped to one line.
- **Call bar:** in push-to-talk or toggle-to-talk, "Talking" (primary colour), else "Not talking: hold Ctrl+Shift+T" / "press …" / "…the gamepad button" / "no key or button is set (Audio…)". Nothing in voice activity.
- **Settings:** the "Push-to-talk arrives in a later version." line is gone (`PUSH_TO_TALK_LINE` removed); the Voice & audio line names voice activity and push-to-talk.
- **`docs/accessibility.md`:** key capture, the Talking controls, and push-to-talk from the keyboard alone.
- **APIs** (rule 2): `iced_core-0.14.0/src/widget/text.rs:445` (`text::primary`); the keyboard types as in step 25. Iced 0.14 buttons take no keys (`iced_widget-0.14.2/src/button.rs` has no keyboard handling), so a captured Space or Enter cannot also press **Set key…**.
- **Mutation:** without the capture guard in `on_shortcut`, Esc closed the card while waiting (1 test failed); reverted.
- **Tests** (12 in `audio_settings::tests::talking`): the mode menu both ways (delay kept, 200 ms from voice activity, bindings kept, an unchanged choice sends nothing); the level switch, slider and steps (the delay slider does nothing in voice activity); the release delay's steps, slider and clamp; capture (modifiers wait, a refused key keeps waiting with the note, the binding stored in the stored syntax, the other binding kept, **Clear**); Esc as a shortcut ignored while waiting and Esc, focus loss, **Cancel** and close all stopping it with nothing saved; capture messages from fresh presses only; the button menu (every button, distinct labels, unknown kept, None, Unknown changes nothing); key labels; source and keyboard lines; the call bar line in each case; the view in each mode, while waiting, with a refused key and with bad bindings; the old window key off while waiting, and source reports shown. The other controls' tests still check that they leave the input mode and bindings alone.

### Step 28: the desktop entry (phase6.28)
- **`packaging/linux/io.github.ga_lep.Twicecat.desktop` (new, Q5):** Type Application, Name Twicecat, `Exec=twicecat`, Categories `Network;InstantMessaging;Chat;`, `StartupWMClass=io.github.ga_lep.Twicecat`, no icon (Phase 7). `desktop-file-validate` passes. `Exec` takes no `%u`, unlike the plan's draft: the app refuses unknown arguments, so a URL would stop it.
- **App ID in one place:** `twicecat_ptt::APP_ID` is now public on every platform. On Linux the window's Wayland app ID is set to it (`iced::window::Settings::platform_specific.application_id`, `iced_core-0.14.0/src/window/settings/linux.rs:5-10`, given through `application(..).window(..)`, `iced-0.14.0/src/application.rs:253`, before `exit_on_close_request`, which edits the same settings), so compositors and the portal tie the window to the entry.
- **`docs/building.md`:** a "Push-to-talk on Linux" section: installing the entry to `~/.local/share/applications/` for the portal during development, the one-time confirmation, X11/Windows/macOS needing nothing, game mode, and the probe.
- **Test:** `the_desktop_entry_carries_the_app_id` (in `main.rs`): the file is named after `APP_ID`, has the keys above, `APP_ID` equals the keychain service name, the window's app ID is `APP_ID` on Linux, the app refuses a URL argument, and `desktop-file-validate` passes where it is installed.

### Step 29: wrap-up (phase6.29)
- **Branch review** by an independent read-only agent over `main..HEAD`. It found 3 defects and 1 likely race, all fixed and folded into the commits that wrote the code (rule 5: `--fixup`, `git rebase --autosquash main`, clean; the final tree identical to the unsquashed one; the app's lib tests run again on both rewritten commits: 1039 and 1051 passed):
  1. *(high) The window never got push-to-talk updates.* Iced builds the subscriptions once at boot, and a stream that ends is never started again under the same ID. The update channel was made only when the forwarding thread started, so the stream taken at boot was empty: no "Talking", no source lines. The channel is now made with the model. The tests had read the channel directly. New test `the_stream_taken_at_boot_carries_later_updates` takes the stream before any service, as Iced does.
  2. *(medium) Waiting for a new key while holding the old one left the microphone open.* The release was dropped while capturing. Now only presses are dropped; the test checks a release lets go.
  3. *(low) The `twicecat-ptt` tree check matched whole names only*, so `ruma-common` or `livekit-api` would have passed. It now matches by prefix, as the app check does.
  4. *(likely race) A late event from a stopped service could turn toggle-to-talk on* (a portal task outlives the service). Stopping a service now retires its generation in the forwarding thread; `a_stopped_service_cannot_start_talking` checks it.
- **Gate:** fmt, clippy (also `--all-features`), `cargo test --workspace` 1730 passed / 0 failed / 151 ignored, and the dependency, API and commit checks: ok.
- **Issue #15's scope, by commit:**
  - start, restart and drop the service from the settings, on the main thread; forward `Transmit` outside `update`; the window fallback; `Ready`/`Unavailable` kept per source; the dependency boundary: `feat(app): run push-to-talk and forward it to the core`;
  - toggle-to-talk: `feat(core): offer toggle-to-talk in the push-to-talk input mode`, and the app's flipping in the commit above;
  - the release tail driven by a fake sink: `feat(app): check the push-to-talk release tail end to end`;
  - the settings UI (mode, key capture, gamepad button, release delay, the current binding and what each source reports) and the state shown in the call bar: `feat(app): set voice activity, push-to-talk or toggle-to-talk in the card`;
  - the `.desktop` file with the app ID: `feat(app): ship a desktop entry with the app ID the portal knows`;
  - backend selection stays covered by `twicecat-ptt`'s tests.
- **Open issues:**
  - (low) Dropping the service joins the hotkey and gamepad threads, which can hold `update` up to about 200 ms when a call ends or a binding changes (step 25).
  - (low, UX) While the card waits for a new key, only the window source is off. The global shortcut (or portal) of the old key still talks, and on X11, Windows and macOS it is grabbed, so pressing the old key again cannot capture it. Rebinding to another key works.
  - The rendering and the real sources are on the checkpoint below.

- [x] Plan questions answered and plan approved (2026-10-13).

On Wayland, after installing the desktop entry (`docs/building.md`), the portal dialog appears once and the card shows the key it reports ("Desktop shortcut: ready, press …").

- [ ] Toggle-to-talk: one press starts, the next stops; leaving the call stops.
- [ ] The Talking section in both themes: the menus, **Set key…** with a chord (Ctrl+Shift+T), a function key and a refused key, Esc while waiting (the card stays open), the source lines, and the call bar's "Talking" / "Not talking: hold …" line.
