## Phase 9 steps (video: camera and screen sharing up to 4K60; plan docs/plans/phase9-video.md, ga-lep/twicecat#22; branch feat/video)
Plan approved by the human 2026-10-13: Q6-Q8 as the human chose them (NVENC/NVDEC through CUDA on NVIDIA Ada and newer; Vulkan Video on Intel, AMD and NVIDIA before Ada; no VA-API; GPU-decoded frames copied to host memory for the app until iced moves to wgpu ≥30), the other questions as recommended (livekit 0.9.3 → 0.9.5; H.264 by default with AV1 opt-in; our own encoders through LiveKit's passthrough source with simulcast added in a webrtc-sys fork; the crates listed in Q11). `feat/video` is cut from `fix/pre-video` (the two pre-phase fixes above), which goes to `main` first.
- [x] 1. Bump livekit and check the logs for key material (phase9.1)
  - **Done:** `livekit` =0.9.3 → =0.9.5 (approved, Q2). `cargo update -p livekit` moved only its own family: libwebrtc 0.3.50 → 0.3.52, webrtc-sys 0.3.47 → 0.3.49, livekit-protocol 0.8.0 → 0.8.1, livekit-common, -data-stream, -datatrack, -rpc and -signaling.
  - **No new prebuilt:** webrtc-sys 0.3.49 still builds with webrtc-sys-build 0.3.19, whose `WEBRTC_TAG` is `webrtc-89d790b` (lib.rs:31). `scripts/fetch-libwebrtc.sh` and its SHA-256 stay as they are.
  - **What changed upstream (read in the registry; the changelogs were not updated):**
    - The passthrough encoder declares `has_trusted_rate_controller = true` (webrtc-sys 0.3.49 `src/passthrough_video_encoder.cpp:350-352`). After a gap before `Encode()`, it holds delta frames until the next keyframe and asks for one (`ContinuesChain`, `:259-265`, `:369-379`). The gap is found from a per-source sequence number that the source sets as it delivers each frame (`src/video_track.cpp:202`).
    - `PeerConnection::set_bitrate(BitrateSettings { min, start, max })` (libwebrtc 0.3.52 `src/peer_connection.rs:81-101`, `:140-154`).
    - livekit 0.9.5: `Room::close` now marks the room disconnected itself, and `Reconnected` is sent only on a real state change (`src/room/mod.rs`). `RefreshPublishingLayers` refreshes video layers under dynacast. `SfuConnection::close` stops its tasks before closing the room, so it is unaffected.
  - **Unchanged and re-checked:** the libwebrtc log sink (libwebrtc 0.3.52 `native/peer_connection_factory.rs:41-43`, webrtc-sys `src/webrtc.cpp`) and webrtc-sys `build.rs:112`. The citations in `call/e2ee.rs` and `dev/libwebrtc/README.md` now name the new versions.
  - **Key logging after the bump:** `call_log_hygiene` passes. The raw libwebrtc log still holds 6 lines with key words in an encrypted call with a rotation (same prebuilt), and 0 libwebrtc lines pass the core's filter at `debug`.
  - **Checks:** fmt; clippy, also with `--all-features`; 1700 tests; `check-app-deps.sh`, `check-core-api.sh`, `check-no-webrtc-download.sh`. Full ignored suite on the local stack with the models (`--no-fail-fast -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`): 156 passed, 0 failed. GPU suites on the RTX 5080: audio 9 passed (`onnxruntime`, `deepfilter`, release), core 10 passed (`call_media`, `gpu_enhancement` with `gpu-enhancement`). `cargo deny` is not installed here; CI runs it (`cargo-deny-action`). The new versions keep their Apache-2.0 licence.
- [x] 2. `twicecat-video` skeleton, frames, test pattern, ladder, API shape (phase9.2): the API shape approved by the human 2026-10-13 (with `CameraPreview` and the whole list in `VideoTracks`); Phase 9 continues while the Phase 6 call UI checkpoint is open (human, 2026-10-13)
  - **`crates/video` (`twicecat-video`):** no Matrix, LiveKit or UI; `yuvutils-rs` =0.8.3 (BSD-3-Clause OR Apache-2.0, Q11), at opt-level 3 in dev builds.
    - `frame`: `CpuFrame` (NV12, I420, YUYV, BGRA; planes checked against format and size when made; `Debug` shows only the shape), `Frame::{Cpu, DmaBuf}` (DMA-BUF on Linux; D3D11 and IOSurface come with Task 12), `ColorSpace` (BT.601/709, limited/full).
    - `convert`: BGRA, YUYV, I420 → NV12; NV12, YUYV, BGRA → I420; NV12, I420 → BGRA. The SIMD conversions are yuvutils' `bgra_to_yuv_nv12`, `bgra_to_yuv420`, `yuyv422_to_yuv420`, `yuv_nv12_to_bgra` and `yuv420_to_bgra` (read in the registry: `src/rgba_to_nv.rs:1466`, `rgba_to_yuv.rs:1370`, `yuy2_to_yuv.rs:364`, `yuv_nv_to_rgba.rs:1203`, `yuv_to_rgba.rs:903`). NV12 ↔ I420 is our own chroma repack (yuvutils has none).
    - `test_pattern`: moving NV12 content and a 96-bit stamp (frame counter, 48-bit send time in µs, CRC-16) in 3 rows of 32 blocks sized from the frame's width. It reads back after ±40 luma noise and after scaling to 1/2 and 1/4, and a damaged stamp reads as none.
    - `ladder`: Q12's bitrates (exact where the plan lists them; otherwise scaled from the nearest listed point by pixel rate^0.75, AV1 × 0.6). `best_layer` drops framerate first for a screen and resolution first for a camera. `layers` adds 1080p30 and 540p15 under a screen, 720p30 and 360p30 under a camera (at most 3). `split` shares WebRTC's total, smallest layers first, and turns off the layers that do not fit.
    - `annexb`: NAL unit parsing (3- and 4-byte start codes), rewriting to 4-byte start codes (emulation prevention untouched), AVCC → Annex B, keyframe detection (IDR + SPS + PPS).
  - **Core API (`api/video.rs`, SDK-free):**
    - Commands: `ListVideoDevices`, `ListScreenSources`, `StartCamera`, `StopCamera`, `StartScreenShare { source, quality, audio }`, `StopScreenShare`, `SetVideoView`, `GetVideoSettings`, `UpdateVideoSettings`, `ProbeVideoEncoders`, `StartCameraPreview`, `StopCameraPreview`. Each answers the new `CoreError::NotSupported("video")` for now.
    - Events: `VideoDevices`, `ScreenSources`, `VideoSettings`, `VideoTracks`, `VideoFeed { track, feed }`, `CameraPreview(VideoFeed)` (not in the plan's list: the preview needs a feed without a track), `LocalVideo`, `VideoEncoders`, `VideoStats`.
    - `VideoFeed` is a latest-frame slot over a `watch` channel: `latest()`, `changed().await` (false once the track ended), `is_ended()`, and `VideoFeed::channel()` for the core and for tests.
    - `VideoDeviceId`, `ScreenSourceId`, window titles and frame bytes stay out of `Debug`.
  - **Call intent:** `CallIntent::Video`. `JoinCall`'s intent now reaches our membership's `m.call.intent` (and its refreshes) and the ring notification, and an incoming ring's `m.call.intent` reaches `IncomingCall.intent` (missing or unknown: audio). Joining for video publishes no video yet.
  - **Boundary checks:** `check-app-deps.sh` also refuses twicecat-video, ash (and ashpd), pipewire and x11rb; `check-core-api.sh` also refuses twicecat-video, ash, ashpd, pipewire, x11rb and yuvutils-rs in the public API. The serializer test in `session.rs` allows `video.rs` (non-secret video settings, like `audio.rs`).
  - **Tests:** 24 in `twicecat-video`; in the core, `Debug` hiding, the feed, settings serde, membership and ring intents, and `video_commands_answer_not_supported_for_now` (`call_settings`).
  - **Checks:** fmt; clippy, also with `--all-features`; 1732 tests; the four boundary scripts. On the local stack: `call_signalling`, `call_ringing`, `call_media` (9), `cli`, `call_log_hygiene` pass.
  - **Found on the way, not fixed here:** `crates/core/src/engine/calls.rs` is tracked but never compiled (no `mod calls`); a separate task was suggested.
- [x] 3. Hardware probe and backend measurement spike (phase9.3): Q6 revisited with the human 2026-10-13 and **kept as it is**: Vulkan Video only for Intel, AMD and NVIDIA before Ada, the software path where no Vulkan encoder with rate control is offered (no VA-API)
  - **Probe (`twicecat_video::probe`):** `probe()` never fails; it lists every Vulkan GPU and adds the NVIDIA part to the GPU with the same UUID. `choose(gpu)` follows Q6, and Vulkan Video counts only with CBR or VBR (WebRTC sets the bitrate). `nvenc_session_limit(n)` opens sessions until the driver refuses. `cargo run -p twicecat-video --example video_probe [-- --sessions N]` prints it all.
    - Vulkan (`probe/vulkan.rs`, `ash` =0.38.0+1.3.281, Q11): the instance at API 1.3, `PhysicalDeviceIDProperties`/`DriverProperties`/`DrmPropertiesEXT`, the video queue families (`QueueFamilyVideoPropertiesKHR`), and `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoCapabilitiesKHR` for H.264 Constrained Baseline and H.265 Main 8-bit 4:2:0 through ash's raw function table (`extensions_generated.rs:10733`). It also checks the DMA-BUF import extensions and `VK_KHR_external_memory_fd`. AV1 encode is only noted by its extension name (newer than ash's headers).
    - NVIDIA (`probe/nvidia.rs`, `libloading` =0.8.9, already in the lock through ash): `libcuda` (`cuInit`, compute capability attributes 75/76, `cuDeviceGetUuid_v2`, contexts, external memory), `libnvidia-encode` (NVENC API 12.1 as the human chose: driver 530.41 / 531.61 or newer; function list, `nvEncOpenEncodeSessionEx` on a CUDA context, encode GUIDs, caps) and `libnvcuvid` (`cuvidGetDecoderCaps`). The layouts come from nv-codec-headers n12.1.14.1 (MIT; downloaded to read, SHA-256 `25bce437…b8f`, not vendored): `nvEncodeAPI.h:126` (`NVENCAPI_STRUCT_VERSION`), `:1162` (caps enum), `:4375` (function list), `dynlink_cuviddec.h:159` (`CUVIDDECODECAPS`). Size tests pin each struct (function list 2552 bytes, open-session 1552, caps 256, decode caps 88).
    - Vulkan → CUDA (Linux): a Vulkan buffer exported as an opaque fd, imported by CUDA, written by CUDA and read back by Vulkan (and the other way round).
  - **What the probe found on this machine (2026-10-13, probe 0.5-0.75 s):**
    - RTX 5080 Laptop (driver 615.78.08, CC 12.0, CUDA 13.4, NVENC API 13.1 on offer): NVENC H.264 up to 4096², H.265 and AV1 up to 8192², 2 engines, dynamic bitrate, 4 temporal layers (AV1 3). The "macroblocks per second" cap (983,040) is level 5.1's MaxMBPS, not throughput. NVDEC H.264 and AV1 up to 8192². **12 NVENC sessions at once** (we need at most 6: 3 layers × camera + screen). **Vulkan → CUDA memory: works.** Vulkan Video also offers H.264 (4096², level 5.2, 120 Mb/s, CBR and VBR) and H.265. Chosen: NVENC.
    - Intel Arrow Lake iGPU (Mesa 26.2.4 ANV): **no Vulkan Video encode by default.** With `ANV_DEBUG=video-encode` it shows H.264 and H.265 up to level 5.1 with **no CBR and no VBR** (constant quantizer only, max bitrate 0). Chosen: none (software path).
  - **Speed reference (`scripts/measure-encoders.sh 30`):** FFmpeg n9.0.2 encodes 30 s of 4K60 testsrc2 NV12 from CPU memory, no pacing, 20 Mb/s target (AV1 12), keyframe every 10 s (Vulkan: one keyframe, see below). "Call" times are FFmpeg's per-call times, an upper bound on what the encoder adds per frame; fps is the number that says whether 4K60 fits.

    | encoder | GPU, driver path | fps | call p50 / p99 ms | CPU % | Mb/s | bitrate |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | `h264_nvenc` (p1, ull, Baseline, CBR) | RTX 5080, NVENC | 306.7 | 0.14 / 4.70 | 106 | 20.01 | +0.1% |
    | `av1_nvenc` (p1, ull, CBR) | RTX 5080, NVENC | 487.7 | 0.01 / 1.62 | 174 | 12.02 | +0.2% |
    | `h264_vulkan` (High, VBR) | RTX 5080, Vulkan Video | 221.1 | 0.30 / 4.56 | 80 | 20.16 | +0.8% |
    | `h264_vulkan` (CQP 26) | Intel ARL, ANV (hidden) | 138.2 | 2.47 / 7.57 | 48 | 71.82 | +259% (no rate control) |
    | `h264_vaapi` (Baseline, CBR), not in the plan | Intel ARL, VA-API (iHD) | 144.6 | 2.48 / 7.02 | 95 | 20.15 | +0.7% |
    | `h264_vaapi` | RTX 5080 | failed: no VA-API driver | | | | |

    CPU % includes FFmpeg reading the clip and uploading 4K frames; our encoder will take frames already on the GPU for screen capture.
  - **FFmpeg caveats on NVIDIA Vulkan Video (FFmpeg's or the driver's, to be checked with our own encoder in Task 4):** Constrained Baseline and Main fail ("Unable to get feedback for H.264 units = 0"), only High runs, although the driver's caps accept Baseline. With High it deadlocks at the second IDR (`-g 600` and `-g 120` both stop there and have to be killed), so the script uses one keyframe for the whole run.
  - **What this means for Q6 (rule 8: brought to the human):** NVIDIA Ada and newer through NVENC: confirmed, with a lot of headroom (5× 4K60 for H.264, 8× for AV1). Intel: **not confirmed.** The only Vulkan encoder on this Arrow Lake is hidden behind a debug flag and has no rate control, while VA-API on the same iGPU does 4K60 at 144 fps with CBR within 0.7%. AMD (RADV, `RADV_PERFTEST=video_encode` on older Mesa) and NVIDIA before Ada could not be measured here (human checkpoint).
    - **The human's answer (2026-10-13): keep Q6.** Intel and AMD GPUs whose drivers offer no Vulkan Video encoder with CBR or VBR (this Arrow Lake on Mesa 26.2 among them) use the software path, which does not reach 4K60; they get hardware encoding when Mesa exposes rate control. VA-API stays out, and we do not drive a constant-quantizer encoder ourselves.
  - **`unsafe` (each with a SAFETY comment):** `probe/vulkan.rs`, 9 (loader, instance, device queries, the caps call, the instance guard); `probe/nvidia.rs`, 48 (symbol lookups, the CUDA, NVENC and NVDEC calls, the session and context guards, the interop test's Vulkan device, buffer, memory, fd export and CUDA import). No `unsafe` in `probe/mod.rs`.
  - **Checks:** fmt; clippy, also with `--all-features`; 1741 tests (31 in `twicecat-video`); the four boundary scripts; `bash -n` on the script (shellcheck is not installed here). The NVIDIA and Vulkan parts run only on hardware, so their unit tests check layouts and the choice policy, and the probe output above is from `video_probe` on this machine.
- [x] 4. Vulkan compute and Vulkan Video H.264 encoder (phase9.4): the acceptance as amended by the human 2026-10-13 (encode on the RTX 5080 through Vulkan Video, conversion and DMA-BUF import on both GPUs); the human accepted the misses below on 2026-10-13, to be tuned in Task 14. **The Vulkan Video encoder was removed on 2026-10-14** (plan amendment: LiveKit's own encoders); the device, conversion, scaling and DMA-BUF import stay, and the encoder's measurements and driver findings below are kept for the record
  - **`twicecat_video::gpu`** (`ash` =0.38.0; WGSL compute shaders in `crates/video/shaders/`, compiled to SPIR-V and validated by `naga` =27.0.3 in `build.rs`, a build-dependency approved by the human 2026-10-13 and already locked through wgpu; the output also passes `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.3`):
    - `GpuDevice::open(uuid)`: our Vulkan 1.3 device with `synchronization2` and a compute queue (the H.264 encode queues went with the encoder). DMA-BUF extensions (`VK_KHR_external_memory_fd`, `VK_EXT_external_memory_dma_buf`, `VK_EXT_image_drm_format_modifier`, `VK_EXT_queue_family_foreign`) are turned on where the driver has them.
    - `Converter`: BGRA (CPU or DMA-BUF) or NV12 (CPU; I420 and YUYV through the CPU converter first) to NV12 at up to three sizes in one submission. A box filter over each output pixel's footprint (up to 8 taps per axis); BT.601/709, limited/full. The shaders write R8 luma and RG8 chroma storage images, then a copy fills the encode picture: on NVIDIA an NV12 encode-source image cannot be a storage image (`vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoFormatPropertiesKHR` with STORAGE gives no format), and `TRANSFER_DST` works on both GPUs.
    - DMA-BUF import (Linux): single-plane XRGB/ARGB/XBGR/ABGR8888 with an explicit modifier, checked importable first (`vkGetPhysicalDeviceImageFormatProperties2` with the modifier and external-memory infos), dedicated import of a duplicated fd, acquired from the foreign queue family in `GENERAL`. Fourcc values from `/usr/include/drm/drm_fourcc.h:193-199,544`. Multi-plane modifiers and YUV DMA-BUFs answer `NotSupported` for now. Waiting for the producer's implicit fence is Task 8's.
    - (removed 2026-10-14) `VulkanEncoder`: up to three layers, each its own H.264 session (Constrained Baseline, `pic_order_cnt_type` 2, one reference, two DPB slots in one two-layer image, no B-frames, IDR on request or when a paused layer resumes). CBR (VBR where there is no CBR), changed by `set_rates`, with a 1 s virtual buffer, full at the start; quality level 0, low-latency tuning, `VkVideoEncodeUsageInfoKHR` with conferencing and desktop/camera hints. SPS (with VUI: colour description, range, `max_dec_frame_buffering` 1) and PPS come from `vkGetEncodedVideoSessionParametersKHR`; each IDR gets them, with 4-byte start codes. Frame-rate decimation per layer by timestamp. The top layer encodes on the first encode queue and the others on the second (NVIDIA's family has two). After a failed submission the layers reset and restart with an IDR.
    - Levels from H.264 Table A-1 (`level_for`), capped by the driver's maximum; `STD_VIDEO_H264_NO_REFERENCE_PICTURE` (0xFF) from Vulkan-Headers v1.3.281 `vk_video/vulkan_video_codec_h264std.h` (read on GitHub). ash's video functions are raw tables (`extensions_generated.rs:10701`, `:15594`); PFN signatures `vk/extensions.rs:4307-4373`, `:6574-6585`; std structures `vk/native.rs`.
  - **Measured on this machine (2026-10-13, release):**
    - Conversion matches yuvutils on both GPUs: luma and chroma within 1 (mean 0.17-0.19). Same-size NV12 output is an exact copy. The test-pattern stamp reads back at 4K, 1080p and 540p.
    - 4K NV12 to three layers with readback: Intel iGPU median 9.1 ms, RTX 5080 11.9 ms (most of it the readback, which the encoder does not do). **Fix found on the way:** Intel's only cached host memory type is not coherent, and reading the coherent (write-combined) one made a 4K readback take 40 ms; buffers now pick their memory by direction (upload: coherent; readback: cached, invalidated before reads).
    - DMA-BUF: each GPU imports its own exported linear buffer and converts it exactly as the same frame from memory. Intel to RTX 5080 also imports. **RTX 5080 to Intel imports but the iGPU's submit fails with `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`** (a buffer in VRAM). Conversion therefore stays on the GPU that owns the capture buffer, and frames go to the other GPU through a CPU copy, as the plan says.
    - **Vulkan Video on the RTX 5080 (forced over NVENC), 4K60 test pattern for 60 s, three layers (4K60 20 Mb/s, 1080p30 4 Mb/s, 540p15 0.8 Mb/s), frames from CPU memory:** encode call p50 5.9-7.3 ms, **p99 7.9-12 ms between runs** (plan: under 8 ms). Layer 0 +0.7%, layer 1 +4.2%, **layer 2 +19.4%** (plan: within 15%). ffprobe reads Constrained Baseline, 3840x2160, level 5.2, 3600 frames. About 1 ms is the CPU upload and about 1.3 ms the conversion and copies; the rest is the 4K encode, and the smaller layers on the second queue add nothing.
    - Layer 2's overshoot: NVIDIA's Vulkan encoder floors at its top QP on this pattern, about 0.96 Mb/s whatever the 540p target below that (1.6 Mb/s is met, 1.58). On the same GPU-scaled frames, FFmpeg's `h264_nvenc` (p1, ull, Baseline, CBR) gives 0.83 Mb/s at 27.7 dB and x264 0.81 at 25.8 dB, against our 0.96 at 23.0 dB. The reference handling is right: a static scene costs 400-600 bytes a P frame, decoded streams show no drift, and a 720p stream at 10.8 Mb/s decodes at 69.7 dB on average. Neither tuning mode, nor quality level, QP limits, GOP hints or deblocking changed this.
    - **Accepted by the human 2026-10-13:** this path is the default only on NVIDIA before Ada (Ada and newer use NVENC, Task 7), it holds the 4K60 frame budget (16.7 ms), and Task 14 tunes it. The hardware test asserts p99 under 16.7 ms and bitrates within -15%..+15% (+25% under 1 Mb/s) from 30 s up.
  - **NVIDIA 615.78 driver findings (Vulkan Video):**
    - With rate control on, every command buffer fails at `vkEndCommandBuffer` with `VK_ERROR_INVALID_VIDEO_STD_PARAMETERS_KHR` unless each rate control layer chains `VkVideoEncodeH264RateControlLayerInfoKHR`, which the spec makes optional. Without it, putting the control in a scope without pictures avoided the error, but the bitrate was ignored (the driver default: 17.2 Mb/s at 4K whatever the target). The validation layer reports nothing in either case.
    - Constrained Baseline works through our encoder, although FFmpeg 9.0.2's `h264_vulkan` fails with it (Task 3).
    - `ULTRA_LOW_LATENCY` tuning is slower than `LOW_LATENCY`: 11.9 against 6.4 ms per 4K60 three-layer frame, for the same bitrate.
    - With a 500 ms initial buffer the first IDR came out at 25 dB; with the buffer full at the start it is 35.6 dB, the same as NVENC's.
  - **Intel iGPU (Mesa 26.2):** no encode (the probe's software path, Q6 kept): `gpus_the_probe_sends_to_software_refuse_to_encode` passes. Conversion, scaling and DMA-BUF import run there.
  - **Validation:** every hardware test also ran with `VK_INSTANCE_LAYERS=VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` (installed by the human for this), with the 4K60 run shortened to 3 s: 0 messages.
  - **Test-pattern note:** making the pattern's chroma move with its luma made 4K harder (26 Mb/s for 20), so the pattern is unchanged. FFmpeg's PSNR filter paired raw H.264 frames by timestamp and shifted them by one; the PSNR figures above compare frames by index.

The encoder's tests (refusal on software-path GPUs, keyframes, the 4K60 three-layer run) were removed with it.

- [x] 5. Publishing and receiving in the core, software path (phase9.5)
  - **Sending (`call/video.rs`, `call/media.rs`):** our camera and screen share are LiveKit video tracks on the connection that publishes, fed raw frames that libwebrtc encodes: VP8, LiveKit's simulcast layers for the size, at most 1080p30 (`software_mode`; `Auto` is the cap). `NativeVideoSource::new` (libwebrtc 0.3.52 `src/video_source.rs:86`), `capture_frame` with an `NV12Buffer` (`video_frame.rs:526-561`), `LocalVideoTrack::create_video_track` (livekit 0.9.5 `room/track/local_video_track.rs:161`), `publish_track` with `video_codec: VP8, simulcast: true` (`room/options.rs:116-175`) and `unpublish_track` (`participant/local_participant.rs:660`). LiveKit's degradation defaults already keep a camera's framerate and a screen's resolution (`options.rs:184-199`). The frames come from the test pattern only, on their own thread at the source's rate, also sent to the track's own feed (self-view, NV12 shared without a copy). Our tracks carry a core-assigned ID, because a full reconnect publishes them again under new SFU IDs (`LocalTrackRepublished`, `room/mod.rs:128-139`, `:1751-1801`). E2EE needs nothing new: the call's key provider covers every track, and our key index reaches the video cryptor on `LocalTrackPublished` as for the microphone.
  - **Receiving:** `RemoteTrack::Video` subscriptions go to the call's registry (`Videos`), shared by every SFU connection. A track is shown only while its sender is an active member, as for audio. Its frames are read with `NativeVideoStream::new` (queue of one, `video_stream.rs:63-71`) on a `twicecat-call-video` thread and copied as I420 (`as_i420`, else `to_i420`, `video_frame.rs:163-201,639-661`) into a `VideoFeed`. `VideoTracks` is sent on every change (tracks, members, muted, picture size), then one `VideoFeed` per new track. Unsubscribing, the sender leaving, our leaving or an SFU closing ends the feeds; leaving also sends an empty `VideoTracks` and a cleared `LocalVideo`.
  - **Layers:** `adaptive_stream` and `dynacast` are on (`room/mod.rs:445-470`). `SetVideoView` sends the tile's size in physical pixels (`RemoteTrackPublication::update_video_dimensions`, `publication/remote.rs:301`) or hides it (`set_enabled`, `:283`; both reach the SFU as `UpdateTrackSettings`, `participant/remote_participant.rs:404-450`). Measured: before any view the SFU sends the lowest layer (320x180), and it moves to 1280x720 within a second of a 1280x720 view.
  - **Mute:** `TrackMuted`/`TrackUnmuted` of a video publication set `VideoTrackView.muted`.
  - **Statistics:** `VideoStats` about once a second while there is a track: from each track's `get_stats()` (`local_video_track.rs:312`, `remote_video_track.rs:206`; fields `stats.rs:347-444`). Sent: the active top layer's size, encode time and quality limitation, bytes over all layers. Received: size, fps and decode time from `frames_decoded` and `total_decode_time`, bitrate, freezes.
  - **API:** `CoreConfig::video: VideoBackend { System, TestPattern }` (new, like `AudioBackend::Virtual`; `TEST_PATTERN_ID`). With the test pattern, `ListVideoDevices` and `ListScreenSources` list it, and `StartCamera`/`StartScreenShare` send it. With the system's devices, cameras and screen sharing answer `NotSupported` until Tasks 8 and 9. `StartScreenShare { audio: true }` answers `NotSupported("screen-share sound")` (Task 8). The settings, the probe and the preview still answer `NotSupported("video")`. `SetUserVolume` gains `source: SoundSource { Voice, ScreenShare }` (plan), `AudioSettings::user_share_volumes`, `Participant::share_volume_percent`. The app sends `Voice` for now; a share-volume control comes with the call stage (Task 10).
  - **Screen-share sound:** played at its own per-user volume, never cleaned, never counted as speaking. Measured: the SFU does not mark a participant speaking for a share's sound alone, so nothing had to be filtered.
  - **CLI:** `--test-video`; `/video` (list), `/video pattern <res> <fps> [screen]`, `/video off [screen]`, `/video view <n> <w>x<h>|hide`, `/video stats`; `/vol <n|user> <pct> [share]`.

  - **Build:** `scripts/fetch-cuda-headers.sh` takes `cuda.h` (CUDA 13.0, `CUDA_VERSION 13000`) from NVIDIA's CUDA runtime wheel already pinned in `scripts/checksums/cuda-runtime.sha256` (about 2 MB, SHA-256 checked) into `~/.cache/twicecat/cuda/headers-13.0.96`, linked into `dev/cuda` (committed directory, like `dev/libwebrtc`). `.cargo/config.toml` sets `CUDA_HOME = dev/cuda` (an environment `CUDA_HOME` wins), so webrtc-sys compiles LiveKit's NVENC/NVDEC (`build.rs:281-313`; the NVIDIA sources include only `<cuda.h>`). Without the header the build goes on without them. NVIDIA's licence grants installing and using the SDK (§1.1.1); the header is a build input only, never shipped. webrtc-sys does not watch `CUDA_HOME`, so CI puts `CUDA_HOME` in rust-cache's key (`env-vars` adds prefixes, `src/config.ts:111-112` of Swatinem/rust-cache v2) and fetches the header in the Linux x86_64 jobs. `docs/building.md` says how.
  - **Encoder choice (`call/encoder.rs`):** LiveKit's `VideoEncoderBackend::list_available()` (`libwebrtc-0.3.52/src/rtp_sender.rs:37-57`) decides; the probe names the GPU. NVENC with H.264 when LiveKit lists it, VideoToolbox on macOS, else `Software` with VP8; `VideoSettings.encoder` can prefer one (software, for example). LiveKit's `Auto`/`Hardware` are never asked for: they would pick VA-API (`video_encoder_factory.cpp`), which crashes here. `ProbeVideoEncoders` lists them (NVENC: H.264 3840x2160@30 and 2560x1440@60; software: VP8 1920x1080@30); `VideoEncoders.in_use` follows the track being sent.
  - **Publishing (`call/media.rs`):** `TrackPublishOptions { video_codec, video_encoder, video_encoding: { top bitrate, max_framerate }, simulcast_layers }` (`livekit-0.9.5/src/room/options.rs:118-145`, presets `:200-240`, `:349-371`): our ladder's layers and bitrates (plan Q12) instead of LiveKit's presets, so 60 fps is kept (LiveKit's 4K preset is 30 fps). Hardware modes: 4K30 (4K60 capped until LiveKit's level fix; `TWICECAT_VIDEO_UNCAPPED=1` lifts the cap), 1440p60 (also `Auto`), 1080p60, 720p30. Software: up to 1080p30, as in Task 5, now with our ladder's bitrates.
  - **Encoder loss:** each sent track on a hardware encoder is watched in the stats loop: lost when LiveKit's `encoderImplementation` stops naming it, or when the source keeps delivering frames (`media-source` `frames`) and no active layer encodes any for 3 reports (dynacast-paused layers and a stalled source do not count). The engine then sends the track again with VP8 (`MediaNotice::EncoderLost`), once, and reports `VideoEncoders` with `in_use: Software`.
  - **A LiveKit NVENC abort, worked around:** the NVENC wrapper returns an error after handing a frame to the callback when the frame can no longer be sent (`webrtc-sys src/nvidia/h264_encoder_impl.cpp:347-352`), and libwebrtc's `SimulcastEncoderAdapter` then pops an empty deque: libc++ aborts the process (core dump backtrace, first hardware run). It happened when we unpublished a track with frames still coming. Our frames now stop, and the encoder gets 150 ms, before a track is unpublished or the room closes. A dynacast layer pause during an encode could still hit it; drafted as upstream issue 6.
  - **Exit:** `Shutdown` already leaves the call before the engine stops. A process that exits with an NVDEC decoder still open hangs in libnvcuvid's exit handler (seen when a failing test skipped its shutdown; upstream issue 5). The app waits for the core's channel to close (Phase 2 Q18), so it exits cleanly unless the core misses its deadline.

The hardware test's DMA-BUF case is Linux only. `cargo clippy -p twicecat-video --all-targets` (with and without `--all-features`) is clean for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `aarch64-apple-darwin` (rustup targets added for this, human 2026-10-14) as well as Linux. `cli::two_clis_send_and_receive_a_test_pattern` read the fps from the first 1280x720 stats, which cover the layer switch (20 fps on the runner). It now reads again for up to 10 s until 25 fps (folded into Task 5); with the bar set out of reach it fails and lists the 11 readings.
  - **After the rebase onto `main` (2026-10-14):** `main`'s push-to-talk app test (`crates/app/tests/ptt.rs`, PR #20) now sets `CoreConfig::video` and unboxes `BridgeEvent::Core`, as the other app tests did since Task 5 (folded into the Task 5 commit). In the CI shards (`scripts/integration-shards.sh`, from `main`'s faster-integration-tests work), `call_video` runs in shard 3, the fastest (588 s in the last run). `call_video_fallback` is in `NOT_SHARDED`, because its only test is a `hardware_` one.
  - **Seen in testing, for Task 14 (recorded with the human 2026-10-14):**
    - The encrypted-call test (720p30 VP8) misses its 28 fps bar in about 1 run in 8: 27.7, 26.6, 27.6 and 26.8 fps, and once 17.4 fps with a 7 s freeze at 23 s and a drop to 360p. The failing runs show bursts of 5-frame losses at 720p in the first 5 s or so.
    - It is not the bitrate. In 10 alternating runs each, our 1.7 Mb/s and 3 Mb/s (VP8 × 1.75, about LiveKit's 720p default) both passed 9 of 10, and both failed in the same minute. Calm runs give 29.6-29.8 fps at 1.7 Mb/s and 30.0-30.1 fps at 3 Mb/s. Task 5's code passed 8 of 8 in one sitting (29.2-29.8 fps).
    - It is not the binary's other test either: that test ends before the watch starts.
    - Where the frames go (SFU, loopback, encoder or receiver) is not found yet. Task 14 reads WebRTC's loss, NACK and PLI counters in a failing run.
    - Once a frame came twice at the 360p→720p switch (now a layer repeat).
    - While the test still picked NVENC, NVDEC logged "Decode Error" at a layer switch and 2 frames were unreadable.
  - **`unsafe`:** one, in a test: `std::env::set_var` in `crates/core/tests/call_video_fallback.rs`, the only test of its binary, before any core starts (SAFETY comment).
  - **Checks:** fmt; clippy, also with `--all-features`; 1772 tests; the four boundary scripts. The ignored suite on the local stack (`--no-fail-fast`): 139 passed, 1 failed. The encrypted-call video test passed in it. The failure was `call_media::a_screen_share_sound_plays_next_to_the_voice`: the share alone came through at 0.0826 for 0.3 sent. It passed when run again, 3 of 3 alone (0.2990) and 9 of 9 as its whole binary, so it is recorded as a one-off (Task 6 does not touch audio). The hardware runs are listed under Acceptance. **After the rebase onto `main`:** fmt; clippy, also with `--all-features`; 1802 tests; the four boundary scripts and `integration-shards.sh --check`. The ignored suite: 139 passed, 1 failed, the intermittent video test above (27.5 fps).
  - **Left for later:** the uplink-shaping check (Task 14, human 2026-10-14); the encrypted-call video test's intermittent frame loss (above, Task 14); NVENC 4K60, VA-API on Intel/AMD, Windows hardware video: upstream fixes (possibly our PR); the NVENC abort on a dynacast pause (upstream issue 6); H.264 start codes from LiveKit's NVENC with Element Call's decryption (browser checkpoint).
